<a id="2026-05-07---backend-settings-patch-risk-summary"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Backend Settings Patch Risk Summary

### Root Problem

The web settings page could locally highlight unknown or changed patch keys, but the backend preview/save result did not include a consistent risk summary for settings that can materially change unattended media behavior. Risk visibility needed to live with the backend command result, not only in frontend rendering.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a versioned `settings_patch_risk_summary.v1` payload to settings patch preview/save results.
- Flags unknown settings keys, removed keys, `AllowNoAudio=true`, `AllowSystemTools=true`, `ReprocessAll=true`, `SizeGuardMode=off/strict`, subtitle drop toggles, MP4 container changes, source/original mutation policy names, and path-root changes.
- Risk items are advisory and are added to backend warnings for medium/high risk settings; existing backend validation remains responsible for hard failures.
- The web patch preview/save detail now renders backend risk-summary items alongside warnings and redacted diffs.

### Validation

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 32 tests passed.
- Ran JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Ran full desktop test suite: 190 tests passed.
- Ran `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Ran V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend startup and shutdown verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.
- Verified live `/api/settings/preview-patch` returns `settings_patch_risk_summary.v1` with high severity for staged `AllowNoAudio=true`, `AllowSystemTools=true`, `SizeGuardMode=off`, and an unknown key.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This changes backend command-result data and warning severity for risky patches, but it does not change persistence semantics or block saves unless the existing validator reports errors.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-local-settings-patch-summary"></a>

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-settings-schema-guidance"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Settings Schema Guidance

### Root Problem

The structured settings patch builder reduced raw JSON editing, but its choices were still duplicated in the static frontend. That allowed schema drift: SizeGuardMode was exposed as `enforce` even though the backend schema accepts `strict`. The builder also lacked visible descriptions explaining what each routing, size, and encoder option does.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto.py`

- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added backend settings field definitions to the read-only settings workspace DTO.
- Updated the web settings builder to refresh select choices from backend schema metadata when available.
- Corrected SizeGuardMode from invalid `enforce` to schema-valid `strict`.
- Added a builder guidance panel that shows selected option descriptions and numeric setting help.
- Preserved unsaved builder edits across the four-second refresh loop instead of resetting controls while the operator is editing.

### Validation

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 31 tests passed.
- Ran JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Ran full desktop test suite: 189 tests passed.
- Ran `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Ran V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.

- Verified live `/api/settings/workspace` returned schema metadata including SizeGuardMode choices `advisory`, `strict`, and `off`.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The settings DTO now includes schema metadata, and the static builder consumes it for choices/help. The patch preview/save contract remains unchanged, but frontend controls now depend on the schema metadata shape.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-guarded-csv-rerun-launch"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Guarded CSV Rerun Launch

### Root Problem

CSV rerun is the highest-risk launch command in the current local API because it stages media files and parks rerun outputs. The backend had already narrowed the API to the conservative policy of copy staging, keep originals, and park returns. The web prototype needed to expose that command only in that constrained form, with no delete/move/replace options.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added a CSV Rerun section to the web Launch page.
- Requires a CSV path before sending a backend command.
- Hardcodes the safe policy payload: `stage_mode=copy`, `original_mode=keep`, `return_mode=park`, `dry_run=false`.
- Shows the policy in read-only form fields and requires browser confirmation before `POST /api/rerun/start`.
- Routes rerun start results into the existing Command Results history and rerun launch detail block.
- Kept unsafe move/delete/replace policies out of the web UI.

### Validation

### Regression Risk

Medium. This adds a guarded frontend path to an existing backend process command that can stage and rerun media. The UI deliberately exposes only the conservative copy/keep/park policy already enforced by the backend.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-guarded-audit-launch"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Guarded Audit Launch

### Root Problem

The backend API already exposed audit launch, but the web prototype had no operator-facing form for it. Audit launch is lower media-risk than CSV rerun because it reports on the library rather than copying or publishing outputs, so it is the next reasonable process command after guarded pipeline start.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added an Audit Launch section to the web Launch page.
- Added library-root, include-sidecars, and show-console controls.
- Pre-fills the audit library root from the read-only settings workspace `outsource` path when available.
- Requires browser confirmation before calling `POST /api/audit/start`.
- Routes audit launch results into the existing Command Results history and audit launch detail block.
- Left CSV rerun launch out of the web UI for now because it needs stricter media-copy/park policy controls.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade`: 20 tests passed.
- Ran workstation Node.js `--check` on `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 178 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This adds a guarded frontend path to an already-tested backend audit launch command. It can start an audit process if confirmed, but it does not alter audit script behavior, pipeline processing, media files, pending publish, or settings persistence.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-guarded-pipeline-launch"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Guarded Pipeline Launch

### Root Problem

The local API already exposed `POST /api/pipeline/start`, but the web prototype intentionally kept launch commands hidden until the command-result pattern, schedule visibility, and operator confirmations were in place. A deeper inspection found a safety mismatch: the Tk controller owns schedule override prompts and stop-at-window-end bookkeeping, while the local API start path did not. Exposing launch buttons before correcting that would let the future web shell bypass schedule enforcement.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\styles.css`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added a backend schedule gate to `MediaPipelineApplicationFacade.start_pipeline_process()`.
- Kept `validate` and `drain_pending_pushes` unscheduled, matching current intent.
- Allowed normal starts when schedule enforcement is off.
- Rejected outside-window starts unless the request explicitly carries `schedule_override=run_once` or `schedule_override=ignore`.
- Blocked scheduled `continuous` starts unless `schedule_override=ignore` is explicit, because the local API does not yet own the Tk schedule-stop watcher that writes Stop After Current at the end of a schedule window.
- Added a Launch page to the static web prototype with mode, sleep seconds, schedule override, show-config/show-console toggles, and a browser confirmation before `POST /api/pipeline/start`.
- Routed launch results into the existing Command Results history and launch detail block.
- Added regression coverage for schedule-gated pipeline starts and the Launch page wiring.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade`: 20 tests passed.
- Ran workstation Node.js `--check` on `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 178 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This intentionally tightens local API process-start behavior when schedule enforcement is enabled, which is safer for the future web shell but could block a previously possible API-only continuous launch. The Tk app path is unchanged and remains the production launcher.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-shell-close-lifecycle-reinforcement"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Shell Close Lifecycle Reinforcement

### Root Problem

The dev-shell smoke exposed that backend cleanup could still race window close. The first harness fix waited longer for Python cleanup, but a later smoke showed the Rust shell needed a stronger close path as well. The shell-owned backend must not survive a normal Tauri window close.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Updated the Tauri window event handler to shut down the backend on both `CloseRequested` and `Destroyed`.
- Explicitly exits the Tauri app after backend shutdown on main-window close.
- Updated the smoke harness to wait for backend cleanup for the configured close timeout rather than checking once after one second.
- Updated the smoke harness to close the actual `mediapipeline-tauri-shell` process, using the titled WebView window only as visibility proof. This avoids accidentally closing a WebView host process without exercising the Tauri close handler.

### Validation

- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1`: passed; `cargo check` completed.
- Parsed `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1 -TimeoutSeconds 120 -CloseTimeoutSeconds 30`: passed.
- Reran the launch smoke after the WebView/shell process selection fix: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 176 tests passed.
- Ran `Build-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1 -DryRun`: passed and reported a 4,286-file V5 deployable destination plan.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This only affects the not-yet-default Tauri shell, but it is process lifecycle code. The intended behavior is stricter cleanup of the shell-owned local API backend; it does not affect the current Tk app or running PowerShell media jobs.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-read-only-maintenance-health"></a>

The future web shell did not expose the existing environment/tool health check. Operators need to see whether PowerShell, FFmpeg, ffprobe, MKVToolNix, optional NVIDIA telemetry, and subtitle conversion dependencies are available without opening the Tk Maintenance tab or searching logs.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-read-only-schedule-workspace"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Read-Only Schedule Workspace

### Root Problem

The future web shell did not expose the app-owned schedule state. The current Tk UI stores schedule enforcement and the weekly 30-minute grid in desktop app state, and process launch logic uses that state to decide whether to start, run once, or request stop after the current file. A replacement shell needs to show the same schedule facts before it can safely own start/stop workflows.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added `ScheduleWorkspaceDto` as a read-only schedule/app-state contract.
- Added `MediaPipelineApplicationFacade.get_schedule_workspace()`, which loads existing app state, normalizes the existing schedule grid, evaluates the current schedule window, and summarizes each day into readable allowed windows.
- Added token-protected read-only `GET /api/schedule` and route-contract metadata with `effect=none`.
- Added a Schedule page to the static web prototype with enforcement state, allowed-now state, next allowed start, current/next window end, status summary, app-state path, warnings, and weekly window summaries.
- Kept editing disabled. This does not save schedule changes or change launch behavior.
- Added regression coverage for persisted app-state schedule parsing, route contract metadata, local API output, served static HTML, and frontend fetch/render wiring.
- Tightened the Tauri dev-shell smoke harness so it waits up to the close timeout for the Python backend to finish graceful shutdown after the Tauri window exits.

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade`: 17 tests passed.
- Ran workstation Node.js `--check` on `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 175 tests passed.
- Parsed `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1 -TimeoutSeconds 120 -CloseTimeoutSeconds 30`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is a read-only DTO/API/page over existing app-state schedule services. It does not persist schedule edits, launch processes, write control flags, or change current Tk schedule enforcement.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-dev-shell-launchclose-smoke"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Dev Shell Launch/Close Smoke

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell could compile, but it still needed a real launch/close verification. The first smoke exposed a lifecycle bug: closing the Tauri window exited the shell while the Python local API backend remained alive.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added `Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`, a real dev-shell smoke harness that starts `npm run dev`, detects the Tauri window, detects the Python `local_api_main` backend child, sends a normal window close, and verifies the backend process is not orphaned.
- Moved backend shutdown from relying only on Rust `Drop` to an explicit Tauri `WindowEvent::CloseRequested` handler.
- Kept `Drop` as a fallback cleanup path.
- Increased the graceful backend shutdown HTTP timeout from 750 ms to 2 seconds for close-path reliability.
- Added release verification for the launch checker script.
- Added tests that assert the Rust scaffold handles `CloseRequested` and that the launch checker validates window/backend cleanup.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 173 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.

<a id="2026-05-07---v5-local-backend-graceful-shutdown"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - V5 Local Backend Graceful Shutdown

### Root Problem

The future Tauri shell could start the Python local API backend, but shutdown still depended on killing the child process when the shell closed. On Windows that can skip Python cleanup paths and make backend lifecycle behavior harder to reason about.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\local_api_main.py`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added token-protected `POST /api/backend/shutdown` with command-result schema `desktop_command_result.v1`.
- Added the shutdown route to the local API contract with side-effect class `backend-lifecycle`.
- Wired `local_api_main` so the shutdown endpoint sets the backend stop event, allowing `server.stop()` and `service.stop_background_tasks()` to run through the existing `finally` path.
- Updated the Tauri scaffold to store backend URL/token and request `/api/backend/shutdown` when the shell-owned backend process is dropped.
- Kept a bounded fallback: if graceful shutdown does not exit within three seconds, the shell still terminates the child process.
- Added tests for route auth/contract metadata, shutdown event signaling, Rust shutdown request markers, and kill fallback markers.

### Validation

- Parsed changed Python modules with `py_compile`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade DesktopApp.tests.test_tauri_shell_scaffold`: 22 tests passed.
- Ran a headless local API shutdown smoke test: backend returned `backend.shutdown` success and exited with code 0.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 170 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Parsed `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`, `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1`, and `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1`: passed. Node, npm, Cargo, and rustc are warnings by default on this workstation; WebView2 runtime was detected by registry hint.
- Ran `Build-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1 -DryRun`: passed and reported 4,276 files copied to a V5 deployable destination plan.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. The endpoint is token-protected and not exposed as a web UI button. It only affects the local API backend process, not the PowerShell pipeline, FFmpeg jobs, media files, queue manifests, pending publish, or config files. The Rust side still needs Cargo validation once the toolchain is installed.

<a id="2026-05-07---v5-tauri-backend-process-pipe-safety"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - V5 Tauri Backend Process Pipe Safety

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell scaffold captured the Python backend's stdout and stderr, but only read the first stdout bootstrap line and did not drain stderr. Once the shell becomes runnable, enough backend output could fill a pipe and block the backend process.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added a backend stdout reader that sends the first bootstrap line to the launcher and continues draining later stdout lines.
- Added a stderr drain thread so backend diagnostics cannot fill the captured stderr pipe.
- Added explicit child termination for bootstrap read errors, bootstrap timeout, invalid bootstrap JSON, unexpected bootstrap schema, and missing captured streams.
- Kept backend ownership unchanged: the shell still starts only `mediapipeline.desktop.local_api_main` and does not launch PowerShell, FFmpeg, or media tooling directly.
- Added scaffold tests covering pipe draining and failed-bootstrap cleanup markers.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_tauri_shell_scaffold`: 6 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 168 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Parsed `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1` and `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1`: passed. Node, npm, Cargo, and rustc are warnings by default on this workstation; WebView2 runtime was detected by registry hint.
- Ran `Build-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1 -DryRun`: passed and reported 4,276 files copied to a V5 deployable destination plan.
- `cargo check` was not run because Cargo/Rust is not installed on this workstation.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. The change is isolated to the not-yet-default Tauri scaffold, but it touches future process lifecycle behavior. Rust compile validation is still required once the toolchain is installed.

### Implementation

- Added versioned DTOs for health, snapshot, telemetry, diagnostics, queue preview, rename preview, settings workspace, and command-result envelopes.
- Added `MediaPipelineApplicationFacade` as a UI-neutral boundary over the existing services.
- Preserved `0%` NVENC encoder utilization as valid telemetry instead of treating it as missing data.

- The scaffold Rust launcher starts `mediapipeline.desktop.local_api_main`, reads `desktop_local_api_bootstrap.v1`, and opens the backend-served web UI rather than duplicating media logic in the shell.

- Added Tauri build artifact exclusions for `node_modules`, Rust `target`, and inactive Cargo lockfile output.
- Added a non-mutating settings validation command that returns the standard command-result envelope without saving PSD1 files.
- Added backend process command envelopes for pause/resume, stop, rescan, pipeline start, audit start, and CSV rerun start.
- Kept launch commands API-only in the static prototype; only pause/resume, stop, and rescan have browser buttons in this pass.
- Guarded launch commands against fresh active pipeline/audit progress and related MediaPipeline PowerShell processes.
- Kept CSV rerun API defaults to real run, `copy`, `keep`, and `park`, and rejected unsafe default delete/replace mode changes for this migration phase.
- Added contract tests for facade serialization, token enforcement, static asset serving, queue preview, rename preview, settings workspace redaction, settings validation, Tauri scaffold integrity, and the local API routes.
- Updated the release builder default deployable name from V4 to V5.
- Updated the release verifier to assert the application facade, local API server, headless API entry point, static web prototype assets, and Tauri scaffold assets are present.

Process routes can write pause/stop/rescan flags and can launch existing pipeline/audit/rerun service paths, so they remain token-protected, localhost-bound, covered by command envelopes, and launch buttons are not exposed in the static prototype yet.

## Current Remediation Status

The targeted remediation phases in this changelog are complete through Phase 11. All current phase entries have validation results recorded, and there are no remaining `Pending for this phase` markers.

Historical deferred notes in earlier entries are retained for audit traceability. Several of those deferrals were later resolved by follow-up phases:

- Native process callback consolidation was addressed by the Phase 2 follow-up.
- Pending publish transaction decomposition was addressed by Phases 3 and 4.
- Pending manifest state/schema drift was addressed by Phase 5.
- Event/progress, ActiveJobs, and control-flag contract drift were addressed by Phases 6 through 9.
- Stale pipeline and audit progress launch cleanup were addressed by Phases 10 and 11.

Longer-term architectural cleanup still remains outside this completed targeted pass: desktop app state-container extraction and broader PowerShell job-context decomposition should stay as future refactor work because they require larger compatibility shims and higher UI/pipeline regression risk.

<a id="2026-05-07---phase-11-stale-audit-progress-launch-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Phase 11: Stale Audit Progress Launch Cleanup

### Root Problem

Audit runs had the same stale-progress visibility problem as pipeline runs: a previous `audit_progress.json` could remain visible after a stopped, killed, or crashed audit until the next audit wrote fresh state. This made the UI look like old audit work was still current during the new launch window.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_constants.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_processes.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\process_lifecycle_controller.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_process_service.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_controllers.py`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `AUDIT_PROGRESS_LAUNCH_CLEANUP_STALE_SECONDS` as a launch-specific stale threshold.
- Added `prepare_audit_runtime_for_launch()` to clear stale previous-run `audit_progress.json` only after launch overlap guards have passed.
- Added `_clear_audit_progress_artifacts()` so audit launch prep deletes only audit progress files and does not touch pipeline progress, pause/stop/rescan flags, or other runtime state.
- Added related-process protection: if a MediaPipeline process is still discoverable, stale audit progress is reported but left in place.
- Integrated audit runtime launch prep into `start_audit()`.
- Added tests for stale audit progress removal, pipeline progress preservation, related-process skip behavior, and controller launch-prep action logging.
- Added regression assertions to keep audit launch cleanup scoped and guarded.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script: passed.
- Ran targeted Python process/controller tests: 92 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 147 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This deletes audit runtime progress files during audit launch preparation, but only when progress is stale beyond the launch threshold and no related process is detected. It does not delete media files, pipeline progress, queue files, control flags, manifests, or pending-publish artifacts.

<a id="2026-05-07---phase-10-stale-progress-launch-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Phase 10: Stale Progress Launch Cleanup

### Root Problem

After a stopped, killed, or crashed run, stale `pipeline_progress.json` files could remain visible until the next run wrote fresh progress. Launch guards correctly avoided blocking on stale progress, but the UI could still show previous-run activity during the new launch window.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_constants.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_processes.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\process_lifecycle_controller.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_process_service.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_controllers.py`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `PIPELINE_PROGRESS_LAUNCH_CLEANUP_STALE_SECONDS` as a longer, launch-specific stale threshold.
- Added `prepare_pipeline_runtime_for_launch()` to clear stale previous-run progress only after launch overlap guards have passed.
- Added `_clear_pipeline_progress_artifacts()` so launch prep deletes only `pipeline_progress.json` artifacts and does not clear pause, stop, or rescan flags.
- Added related-process protection: if a MediaPipeline process is still discoverable, stale progress is reported but left in place.
- Integrated runtime launch prep into standard pipeline starts and single-file starts before control-flag preparation.
- Added tests for stale state-store and legacy progress removal, flag preservation, related-process skip behavior, and controller action logging.
- Added regression assertions to keep launch prep scoped to stale progress cleanup rather than broad runtime deletion.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script: passed.
- Ran targeted Python process/controller tests: 89 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 144 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This deletes runtime progress files during launch preparation, but only when progress is stale beyond the launch threshold and no related process is detected. It does not delete media files, queue files, control flags, manifests, or pending-publish artifacts.

<a id="2026-05-07---phase-9-activejobs-runtime-reconciliation"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Phase 9: ActiveJobs Runtime Reconciliation

### Root Problem

ActiveJobs records could remain in `launching` or `active` after the desktop app crashed, lost its process handle, or was restarted after a child process had already exited. That left diagnostics implying a live launch even when the tracked PID was gone.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\active_job.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_processes.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_status.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_process_service.py`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added the `orphaned` ActiveJobs status for crash/lost-handle reconciliation.
- Added `reconcile_active_job_records()` to mark only definitely dead tracked PIDs as orphaned.
- Left ambiguous process-inspection cases untouched, including missing `psutil` or access-denied process details.
- Invoked ActiveJobs reconciliation during snapshot building before diagnostics/status summaries format the record list.
- Added targeted tests for missing-PID orphaning and terminal-record non-mutation.
- Added regression assertions so ActiveJobs reconciliation stays connected to status snapshots.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script: passed.
- Ran targeted Python contract/process/status tests: 28 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 142 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. The change mutates diagnostics state records, not media files or pipeline outputs. It only marks records orphaned when the tracked PID is definitely gone; live or ambiguous records remain unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-07---phase-8-control-flag-contract-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Phase 8: Control Flag Contract Hardening

### Root Problem

Pause, stop, and rescan requests were written as structured JSON, but the shape lived only in `service_processes.py` conventions and PowerShell tolerated any existing file as an active operator request. That made request IDs, timestamps, and action names easy to drift while the UI still appeared to work.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\control_flag.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_constants.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_processes.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_process_service.py`
- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_control_flag.schema.json`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `ControlFlagRecord`, the explicit Python contract for `pipeline_control_flag.v1`.
- Added a JSON schema for current pause/stop/rescan flag payloads.
- Routed desktop control-flag creation and write verification through `ControlFlagRecord.from_mapping()`.
- Routed launch-time stale cleanup reads through the same contract for current-schema files.
- Preserved legacy pipeline semantics: invalid or legacy files still count as present flag files for PowerShell readers and launch cleanup decisions.
- Added targeted tests for control-flag creation, pause toggling, timestamp-based stale cleanup, invalid-current fallback behavior, and unknown action rejection.
- Added reliability assertions so the control flag contract, schema, desktop writer, and PowerShell observation path stay aligned.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script: passed.
- Ran targeted Python contract/process-service tests: 20 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 139 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. Desktop-created control flags are now stricter, but only the known Pause/Stop/Rescan actions are valid. Legacy and invalid existing files still behave as request files so an operator control is not silently ignored.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-7-activejobs-contract-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 7: ActiveJobs Contract Hardening

### Root Problem

Desktop process launches persisted ActiveJobs JSON as ad hoc dictionaries. The same records drive launch diagnostics, crash-recovery visibility, and operator status summaries, but neither the writer nor the diagnostics reader enforced a versioned shape. A malformed current-schema record could look normal in the UI or drift away from the process lifecycle semantics.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\active_job.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_constants.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_processes.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_status.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_status_service.py`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `ActiveJobRecord`, the explicit Python contract for `desktop_active_job.v1` launch records.
- Routed ActiveJobs writes through `ActiveJobRecord.from_mapping()` before atomic persistence.
- Kept corrupt/partial update recovery by rebuilding required defaults from the record path and process handle before writing terminal status.
- Routed diagnostics ActiveJobs summaries through the same contract.
- Preserved legacy no-schema ActiveJobs read tolerance, while invalid current-schema records now surface as `invalid active job contract`.
- Added Python tests for valid launch/terminal records, unknown status rejection, and diagnostics rendering.
- Added reliability assertions so ActiveJobs validation stays enforced at write and read boundaries.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script: passed.
- Ran targeted Python contract/status tests: 19 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 133 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. Newly written ActiveJobs records must match the explicit status and field contract. Legacy no-schema records remain visible, but bad current-schema records now show as diagnostics problems instead of being formatted as normal jobs.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-6-event-and-progress-contract-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 6: Event and Progress Contract Hardening

### Root Problem

The desktop app had a `PipelineEvent` contract, but `read_pipeline_events_tail()` returned raw JSONL objects directly to Live status and Diagnostics. `pipeline_progress.json` had a JSON schema but no Python contract at all. That left two high-traffic UI inputs vulnerable to silent drift: malformed event records could drive status text, and malformed progress files could be treated as valid runtime state.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\progress.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\pipeline_events.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_status.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_status_service.py`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `ProgressState`, a Python contract for `pipeline_progress.json`.
- Normalized and validated progress fields before UI/status code sees them, including required `ProgressVersion`, `LastUpdate`, `Status`, non-negative counters, boolean control flags, and `CurrentStagePercent` in the schema range.
- Added `PipelineEvent.to_mapping()` and routed `read_pipeline_events_tail()` through `PipelineEvent.from_mapping()`.
- Invalid pipeline event records are skipped with a warning; corrupt JSON and non-object JSONL rows remain tolerated by the lower-level tail reader.
- Kept controller-facing return types as dictionaries, so UI/controller code does not need a migration yet.
- Added Python tests for progress normalization/rejection and event-tail contract filtering.
- Added reliability assertions so the desktop status service cannot regress to raw event/progress ingestion.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script and progress JSON schema: passed.
- Ran targeted Python contract/status tests: 15 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 129 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. UI status now ignores invalid current-schema event/progress records instead of rendering them. This is intentional, but if an external tool writes incomplete synthetic progress/events, the app will treat those records as invalid until they conform to the documented contracts.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-5-pending-manifest-contract-drift-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 5: Pending Manifest Contract Drift Hardening

### Root Problem

The pending-publish remediation introduced and preserved manifest states that the desktop and JSON schema did not fully pin down. PowerShell can write `parked_recovered` during crash recovery and `retry_sidecar_failed` when media copy succeeds but sidecar write fails, but the pending manifest JSON schema did not list those states. The desktop Pending Publish scanner also read current-schema manifests as raw dictionaries, so current contract drift could be silently rendered as normal inventory.

### Files Changed

- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_pending_push_manifest.schema.json`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\pending_publish.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_pending_publish.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_pending_publish_service.py`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `parked_recovered` and `retry_sidecar_failed` to the pending manifest schema enum.
- Added `PENDING_PUSH_MANIFEST_STATES` to the Python pending-publish contract and made current-schema manifests reject unknown states.
- Kept no-schema legacy manifests visible in the desktop Pending Publish scanner for backward compatibility.
- Made the desktop scanner validate current `pending_push_manifest.v1` files through `PendingPushManifest.from_mapping`; invalid current manifests now render as `invalid_contract` health rows instead of silently appearing healthy.
- Added tests that keep the Python state set and JSON schema enum exactly aligned.
- Added regression checks so PowerShell writers, JSON schema, Python contracts, and desktop inventory stay synchronized.

### Validation

- Parsed the changed PowerShell regression script and pending manifest JSON schema: passed.
- Ran targeted Python contract/pending-publish tests: 13 tests passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 124 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. Current-schema pending manifests with unknown states now surface as health issues instead of normal rows. Legacy manifests without `schema_version` remain tolerated so existing parked outputs are still visible to the operator.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-4-pending-drain-transaction-isolation"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 4: Pending Drain Transaction Isolation

### Root Problem

`Modules\PendingPush.ps1` still contained the full pending-publish drain transaction after Phase 3: server-copy validation, tx3g sidecar publication, publish partial creation/removal, sidecar backup/restore, media reveal, completed-manifest append, output summary generation, and local artifact deletion. That made the public drain loop hard to reason about because iteration/stop handling and data-loss-sensitive file mutation lived in the same function.

### Files Changed

- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingTransactions.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPublishIndex.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Moved pending helper ownership into `PendingTransactions.ps1`, including transaction IDs, safe file-length reads, tx3g pending sidecar publish records, pending sidecar repair, server-copy validation, and `Repair-PendingManifestState`.
- Added `New-PendingDrainSidecarExtra` to centralize sidecar metadata reconstructed from pending manifests during drain.
- Added `Remove-PendingDrainLocalArtifacts` so successful drain cleanup has one owner.
- Added `Invoke-PendingDrainTransaction` as the per-manifest transaction for copy, sidecar publish, sidecar write, media reveal, retry-state updates, and final cleanup.
- Reduced `Invoke-RetryPendingPushes` to the public drain facade: manifest enumeration, deferred-publish gate, stop handling, progress item context, transaction invocation, structured `publish_drained` events, index refresh, and recovered-count reporting.
- Updated structural regression checks so drain/retry mechanics cannot drift back into `PendingPush.ps1`.

### Validation

- Parsed changed PowerShell files with `System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser`: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 119 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-high. The behavior is intended to be unchanged, but the touched path decides when parked media, parked SRT files, partial publishes, sidecar backups, and manifests can be deleted. Existing regression coverage validates legacy drains, tx3g sidecar drains, missing sidecar failure persistence, missing media payload preservation, failed network copy retry, retry success cleanup, pending_move crash recovery, and existing-server-copy validation.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-3-pending-park-transaction-isolation"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 3: Pending Park Transaction Isolation

### Root Problem

`Modules\PendingPush.ps1` still owned too much of the pending-publish system: public parking APIs, drain/retry orchestration, operator logging, structured events, index refresh, manifest creation, sidecar parking, media moves, and partial-failure cleanup. That density made the most data-sensitive path in the pipeline hard to audit without changing behavior.

### Files Changed

- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingTransactions.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\entrypoints\MediaPipeline.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `PendingTransactions.ps1` as the owner for durable park transaction mechanics.
- Extracted pending sidecar manifest-entry construction into `New-PendingParkSidecarEntries`.
- Extracted current-schema pending manifest construction into `New-PendingParkManifest`.
- Extracted the park transaction into `Invoke-PendingParkTransaction`, including sidecar parking, manifest write/round-trip validation, media move, parked-state update, and pre-move cleanup on failure.
- Kept `Invoke-ParkPendingPush` as the public compatibility wrapper for callers, logging, pending-index refresh, and `publish_parked` event emission.
- Updated module load order so manifest storage loads first, transaction mechanics load second, and the public pending-push wrapper/drain module loads third.
- Added structural regression checks that prevent the park transaction from drifting back into `PendingPush.ps1`.

### Validation

- Parsed changed PowerShell files with `System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser`: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"` with bytecode disabled: 119 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. The public API and pending manifest schema are preserved, but the data-loss-sensitive parking path now crosses an additional module boundary. Existing regression coverage verifies successful parking, manifest validation, local-output preservation on manifest failure, and cleanup of partial sidecar parks.

<a id="2026-05-06---rename-tv-folder-season-inference-fix"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Rename TV Folder Season Inference Fix

### Root Problem

The Rename tab and pipeline naming preview handled plain folder titles like `Show Season 2`, but several real-world folder-title variants still fell back to default `S01`: dotted separators, spaced `S 2`, dotted `S02`, bracketed release folders, trailing `Complete`, and ordinal names such as `2nd Season`.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_rename.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_rename_service.py`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Naming.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added flexible folder-title normalization before season extraction in both Python rename preview fallback and PowerShell pipeline naming.
- Preserved filename season as the highest authority.
- Preserved default `S01` only when no explicit season token appears in the filename or folder path.
- Added support for folder-title forms such as `Show.Season.2`, `Show S 2`, `Show.S02`, `[Group] Show Season 2 [1080p]`, `Show Season 2 Complete`, and `Show - 2nd Season`.
- Added support for release-tagged season folders such as `Serial Experiments Lain 1998 Season 02 1080p BluRay FLAC 2.0 x264-Chotab`; these now produce `S02` while a clear filename show prefix like `Serial Experiments Lain E01 ...` remains the show-title source.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-2-follow-up-callback-capable-native-process-runner"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 2 Follow-Up: Callback-Capable Native Process Runner

### Root Problem

The original Phase 2 remediation added shared native-process contracts and safer timeout defaults, but intentionally left `Invoke-NativeCommand` and `Invoke-FFmpegWithProgress` on separate process loops. That meant FFmpeg still owned its own `ProcessStartInfo`, stderr read loop, stop polling, timeout handling, and kill/drain behavior while other tools used the generic native wrapper.

### Files Changed

- `Pipeline\Modules\Native.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\FfmpegProgress.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `Invoke-NativeProcess`, a shared callback-capable runner for native tools.
- Moved process start, argument-array launch, stdout/stderr line draining, timeout kill, stop-flag polling, bounded drain, and result metadata into the shared runner.
- Kept `Invoke-NativeCommand` as the compatibility API, now backed by `Invoke-NativeProcess`.
- Rebuilt `Invoke-FFmpegWithProgress` as a progress-specific wrapper around `Invoke-NativeProcess` using a stderr-line callback for FFmpeg progress parsing.
- Preserved FFmpeg progress events, repro command behavior, stderr tailing, `LastFFmpeg*` compatibility variables, pause notification, timeout markers, and global process-handle cleanup.
- Made `Native.ps1` self-load `NativeProcessContracts.ps1` when standalone tooling dot-sources it outside the main pipeline module order.

### Validation

- Parsed changed PowerShell modules and reliability test script with `System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser`: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ToolIntegrationChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"`: 118 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. The public function names and return shape are preserved, but FFmpeg now relies on the shared process loop. The regression suite covers success, failure, timeout, start failure, repro command generation, progress updates, and bundled FFmpeg integration.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-1-subtitle-default-language-policy"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 1: Subtitle Default-Language Policy

### Root Problem

The FFmpeg and mkvmerge subtitle builders hardcoded English (`eng`/`en`) as the default subtitle language. That conflicted with the configurable subtitle language policy and folder-level sidecar overrides, especially for operators who choose a non-English preferred subtitle language.

### Files Changed

- `Pipeline\Modules\Subtitles.Common.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Subtitles.Builders.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added shared subtitle-default helpers that derive preferred defaults from `Get-SubtitleLanguagePolicy`.
- Kept undefined and blank language tracks as fallback-only candidates, so they do not steal default disposition ahead of a configured real language.
- Routed both mkvmerge and FFmpeg subtitle default selection through the shared helper.
- Preserved existing behavior where supplemental/signs tracks do not become default unless no better candidate exists.

### Validation

- Added regression coverage proving configured preferred language beats earlier undefined and English tracks in both FFmpeg and mkvmerge builders.
- Preserved existing undefined-before-English regression coverage.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1` with bundled PowerShell 7.6.0: passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. Existing English defaults remain unchanged when `SubKeepLanguages` contains `eng`/`en`. Behavior changes only when the configured preferred subtitle language is non-English or folder policy overrides subtitle language lists.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-2-version-contracts-pending-publish-health-and-safer-runtime-defaults"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 2: Version Contracts, Pending Publish Health, and Safer Runtime Defaults

### Root Problem

Several audit findings were related rather than isolated: the app displayed a product version that did not match V4, persisted records only carried the sidecar compatibility version, pending-publish payload loss could be invisible outside logs, native tool timeout defaults were scattered and sometimes unbounded, CSV rerun arguments exposed unsafe modes that were later ignored, and config backup files were written next to the live PSD1. These issues made the system harder to diagnose and easier to misread during unattended Windows operation.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_config.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_pending_publish.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_pending_publish_service.py`
- `ops\pipeline\entrypoints\Audit-MediaLibrary.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\entrypoints\Invoke-RerunCsv.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\entrypoints\MediaPipeline.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\FfmpegProgress.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Logging.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Native.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Sidecar.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Versioning.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_completed_job.schema.json`
- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_pending_push_manifest.schema.json`
- `ops\pipeline\config\src\mediapipeline\contracts\schemas\media_pipeline_pipeline_event.schema.json`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Added `Pipeline\Modules\Versioning.ps1` so product version, sidecar schema version, and audit schema version are explicit contracts instead of a single overloaded value.
- Set the desktop product version to `v4.000` while preserving sidecar compatibility version `1.0`.
- Added `product_version` to sidecars, pending-publish manifests, pipeline events, and related JSON schemas without changing existing `pipeline_version` compatibility semantics.
- Updated pipeline and audit startup logging so operators can see both product version and schema/compatibility version.
- Added pending-publish health accounting for missing payloads and unreadable manifests in the PowerShell pending index.
- Surfaced pending-publish health rows in the desktop pending-publish service so missing parked payloads are visible to the UI instead of being hidden as only log noise.
- Centralized default native-tool timeouts with named defaults for `ffprobe`, `ffmpeg`, `mkvmerge`, Python helpers, and BDPGS OCR.
- Routed FFmpeg, FFmpeg progress, mkvmerge, and Python tool wrappers through those named defaults.
- Simplified CSV rerun CLI options to expose only the actually supported safe policies: copy-only staging, keep originals, and park outputs.
- Moved future desktop config backup files into `ConfigBackups` beside the active config file instead of mixing timestamped backups into the live config directory.

### Validation

- Parsed all changed PowerShell scripts with `System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser`: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1` with bundled PowerShell 7.6.0: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"`: 111 tests passed.
- Ran Python compile checks for changed desktop modules and tests: passed.
- Smoke-tested Python version exports: `APP_VERSION = v4.000`, `PIPELINE_SIDECAR_VERSION = 1.0`.
- Smoke-tested PowerShell version contract export: `ProductVersion = v4.000`, `PipelineSidecarVersion = 1.0`, `AuditSchemaVersion = 1.0`.

### Regression Risk

Medium. The product-version split is intentionally compatibility-preserving, but any external automation that assumed `pipeline_version` was the user-facing app version should now read `product_version`. Native tool wrappers now have finite default timeouts; the FFmpeg default is deliberately long, but extremely long encodes may need an explicit override if they exceed it.

### Intentionally Deferred

- Full process-runner consolidation remains deferred because merging all subprocess call paths at once would be behavior-changing around FFmpeg progress, native stderr capture, and cancellation.
- Full pending-publish transaction decomposition remains deferred because drain/retry semantics are production-sensitive and should be extracted behind compatibility wrappers.
- Desktop app state-container extraction remains deferred because the current `app.py` controller still owns many UI bindings; splitting it safely requires smaller controller-by-controller migrations.
- Pipeline job context and stage decomposition remain deferred because PowerShell ambient state is widespread and should be reduced by staged adapter extraction rather than a broad rewrite.

<a id="2026-05-06---phase-3-pending-publish-module-split-and-native-process-contracts"></a>
## 2026-05-06 - Phase 3: Pending Publish Module Split and Native Process Contracts

### Root Problem

The audit identified two related maintainability risks that could not be fixed safely with a rewrite-sized change: `PendingPush.ps1` was acting as a dense manifest store, health index, duplicate-detection index, crash-repair helper, park transaction, and drain transaction all at once; and native process execution shared behavior by convention rather than by an explicit result/timeout contract. Both areas were production-sensitive, so this pass split stable helper ownership without changing live publish/drain or FFmpeg progress semantics.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\completed_job.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\pending_publish.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\contracts\pipeline_events.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_contracts.py`
- `ops\pipeline\entrypoints\MediaPipeline.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\Native.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\NativeProcessContracts.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingManifestStore.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPublishIndex.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`

### Implementation

- Extracted pending manifest accessors, manifest read/write, round-trip validation, retry-state persistence, and tx3g sidecar failure persistence into `Pipeline\Modules\PendingManifestStore.ps1`.
- Extracted pending publish index construction, health-row collection, refresh/reconciliation, and already-parked duplicate detection into `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPublishIndex.ps1`.
- Kept `Invoke-ParkPendingPush`, `Repair-PendingManifestState`, sidecar publish, and `Invoke-RetryPendingPushes` in `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1` so park/drain behavior stayed stable.
- Routed raw manifest reads in pending park/index/drain paths through `Read-PendingManifestFile`.
- Added structural regression checks proving manifest persistence no longer lives in `PendingPush.ps1`, and pending index/health/match ownership lives in `PendingPublishIndex.ps1`.
- Added `Pipeline\Modules\NativeProcessContracts.ps1` for `New-NativeCommandResult`, native failure-code classification, external tool result metadata mutation, and named default timeout policy.
- Updated `Invoke-NativeCommand` to return `New-NativeCommandResult`, preserving the existing `ExitCode`, `Output`, `Error`, `Stdout`, `Stderr`, `TimedOut`, `Stopped`, and `ErrorCode` fields.
- Left `Invoke-NativeCommand` and `Invoke-FFmpegWithProgress` as separate runners for now; this pass only centralized shared contracts and timeout policy.
- Updated Python persisted-record contracts to parse optional `product_version` from pending-publish manifests, pipeline events, and completed sidecar/job records.

### Validation

- Parsed all changed PowerShell scripts with `System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser`: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1` with bundled PowerShell 7.6.0 after each extraction group: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -p "test_*.py"`: 111 tests passed.
- Ran focused Python contract tests: 7 tests passed.
- Ran Python compile checks for changed contract modules and tests: passed.
- Ran release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This was mostly extraction and contract surfacing. The highest-risk path touched was native result construction, but the returned field names and values were preserved and covered by the reliability harness. Pending publish behavior is intended to be unchanged; the park/drain transaction functions were deliberately left in place.

### Intentionally Deferred

- Full pending park/drain transaction-object extraction remains deferred. The next safe step is to isolate `Invoke-ParkPendingPush` into a `PendingParkTransaction` wrapper while keeping the current function as the public API.
- Full FFmpeg progress runner merge remains deferred. The progress runner reads stderr line-by-line for progress and bounded diagnostics, so merging it with the generic async-capture runner needs a callback-capable runner and dedicated timeout/stop tests.
- Desktop `app.py` state extraction remains deferred until controller ownership can be migrated in smaller UI-safe slices.
- PowerShell `PipelineJobContext` introduction remains deferred because the main remux/encode paths still rely on ambient `$script:*` state across many modules.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-shell-app-level-backend-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Shell App-Level Backend Cleanup

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell already requested Python backend shutdown during main-window close and destroy events, but the launch/close smoke exposed an intermittent orphaned backend when the shell process exited without the window hook being sufficient. A shell-owned backend must be cleaned up deterministically, otherwise each test or operator close can leave hidden localhost API processes running.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Changed the Tauri shell from shorthand `Builder::run()` to explicit `build()` plus `app.run(...)`.
- Added app-level cleanup for `RunEvent::ExitRequested` and `RunEvent::Exit`.
- Kept the existing `CloseRequested`, `Destroyed`, and `Drop` cleanup paths as independent fallbacks.
- Centralized backend-state shutdown lookup so every lifecycle path uses the same child-process cleanup behavior.

### Validation

- Ran `cargo check` with the user-scope Rust toolchain: passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`: passed; the harness opened the dev shell, detected the actual Tauri process and backend PID, closed the shell, and verified backend cleanup.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds another lifecycle cleanup path and preserves the existing graceful shutdown and force-kill fallback. The only behavior change is more deterministic backend termination when the Tauri shell exits.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-settings-patch-preview"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Settings Patch Preview

### Root Problem

The web Settings page could show a redacted config snapshot and validate visible values, but exposing a direct save endpoint would be unsafe because sensitive values are intentionally rendered as `<redacted>`. Posting the visible table back as a full config could overwrite real secrets or token-like fields. The next safe migration step is a backend-owned patch preview that proves the future save contract without writing PSD1 files.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added token-protected `POST /api/settings/preview-patch` with `effect=none`.
- The endpoint accepts explicit `changes` and optional `remove_keys`, merges them with the backend's unredacted `resolved.config_data`, validates the merged result, and returns a `desktop_command_result.v1` envelope.
- Sensitive keys reject the literal `<redacted>` placeholder so the future save path cannot accidentally persist display placeholders.
- Returned diff data is redacted before it reaches the frontend; the command reports `writes_config=false`.
- Added a Settings page `Patch Preview` panel with JSON input and redacted diff rendering.

### Validation

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 22 tests passed.
- Ran full desktop test suite: 180 tests passed.
- Ran JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Ran `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Ran V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup verified.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is non-mutating and does not save PSD1 files. The main risk is operator confusion because the panel accepts raw JSON; it is intentionally labeled as preview-only until structured settings editors and a true backend patch-save contract are implemented.

<a id="2026-05-07---local-api-settings-reload-from-disk"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Local API Settings Reload From Disk

### Root Problem

The local API backend resolved config paths and config values once at startup. That was acceptable for read-only prototype work, but it becomes stale as soon as the PSD1 changes on disk, and it is a blocker for any future safe settings-save endpoint. The backend needs an explicit way to refresh its resolved state without restarting the shell.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\local_api_main.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `BackendResolvedState` to own the local API's current `ResolvedPaths` behind a lock.
- Added token-protected `POST /api/settings/reload` with `effect=none`.
- The reload command re-runs the existing path/config resolution, updates the backend's resolved-state cache, and returns a command-result envelope with key count and path metadata.
- Added a Settings page `Reload From Disk` button that calls the backend reload command and refreshes the web snapshot.
- Kept the operation non-mutating: it does not write config, touch media, or start pipeline work.

### Validation

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 22 tests passed.
- Ran JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Ran full desktop test suite: 180 tests passed.
- Ran `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Ran V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup verified.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes the headless local API from a fixed resolved snapshot to an explicitly reloadable one. Normal requests still use the cached resolved object, so the global refresh loop does not repeatedly import the PSD1 through PowerShell.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-guarded-selected-rename-apply"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Guarded Selected Rename Apply

### Root Problem

The web Rename page could preview selected-row final names and force-policy overrides, but it still had no way to execute the existing standalone rename transaction. Exposing a generic apply endpoint or accepting frontend-supplied plan rows would be unsafe because a browser-side stale plan could rename more files than intended or bypass backend collision checks.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added token-protected `POST /api/rename/apply` with `effect=filesystem-mutation`.
- The endpoint requires `confirm_apply=true`.
- The endpoint requires explicit `selected_sources` and applies only rows whose source paths are selected.
- The backend rebuilds the rename plan from the current request and existing rename planner; it does not accept prebuilt row objects from the browser.
- Blocked rows are rejected before mutation.
- The actual filesystem operation uses the existing `apply_rename_path_plan()` transaction, undo manifest, collision checks, sidecar moves, metadata updates, and rollback path.
- Added a web `Apply Selected Rename` button with browser confirmation.
- After success, the web prototype replaces applied source paths in the path textarea with returned destination paths and refreshes preview.
- Deliberately did not expose Apply All in this pass.

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 26 tests passed.

- Ran `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.

- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This is the first web prototype command in this migration that can rename media files and sidecars. Risk is constrained by token auth, browser confirmation, backend plan rebuild, selected-source filtering, blocked-row rejection, and the existing transactional rename/rollback service. Apply All remains intentionally unavailable in the web prototype.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-guarded-settings-patch-save"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Guarded Settings Patch Save

### Root Problem

The web Settings page had read-only config rendering, validation, reload, and non-mutating patch preview, but no way to persist a controlled settings change. A full table save would be unsafe because the UI displays redacted sensitive values and could overwrite secrets. The next safe step is a backend-owned explicit patch save, not a frontend-owned config document writer.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added token-protected `POST /api/settings/save-patch` with `effect=config-write`.
- The endpoint accepts explicit `changes`, optional `remove_keys`, and requires `confirm_save=true`.
- The backend merges changes with the unredacted resolved config, preserving sensitive values not included in the patch.
- Sensitive keys reject the literal `<redacted>` placeholder.
- Validation runs before save.
- Save uses the existing `serialize_psd1_document()` and `save_config_document()` path, including backup creation, PowerShell syntax validation, and atomic write behavior.
- After a successful save, the local API reloads resolved paths/config data so the web shell no longer serves stale settings.
- Added a guarded `Save Patch` button to the web Settings patch panel with browser confirmation and command-result logging.

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 27 tests passed.
- Ran JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Ran full desktop test suite: 185 tests passed.

- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.

### Regression Risk

Medium-high. This route can write the active PSD1 config. Risk is constrained by token auth, explicit browser/backend confirmation, backend merge with unredacted config, rejection of redacted placeholders for sensitive keys, existing config validation, backup creation, atomic write, and immediate backend reload. The UI still exposes a raw JSON patch editor; a structured settings editor should replace this before the web shell becomes the default app.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-completed-manifest-backfill-dry-run"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Completed Manifest Backfill Dry Run

### Root Problem

Completed history depends on the local completed-jobs manifest, and the Tk app has a Backfill button that can rebuild it by walking outsource sidecars. The web prototype needed visibility into that recovery workflow without exposing the real manifest rewrite. The existing PowerShell `-DryRun` path avoids manifest writes, but its default checkpoint path is inside completed-state; the web dry-run should keep its checkpoint separate from normal state.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_completed.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added optional `checkpoint_path` support to `CompletedJobsServiceMixin.backfill_completed_manifest()`.
- Added `MediaPipelineApplicationFacade.run_completed_backfill_dry_run()`.
- Added token-protected `POST /api/maintenance/completed-backfill-dry-run` with `effect=process-dry-run`.
- The backend always calls the existing backfill service with `dry_run=True`.
- The web route writes its dry-run checkpoint under `apps\desktop\runlogs` instead of using the normal completed-state checkpoint path.
- Added a Maintenance page `Run Backfill Dry Run` action with result detail for sidecars ingested, skipped bad JSON, manifest path, checkpoint path, stdout, and `writes_manifest=no`.

### Validation

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 29 tests passed.

- Ran full desktop test suite: 187 tests passed.
