- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This starts a bounded PowerShell dry-run that can walk the outsource tree, so it may be slow on a network share. It is forced to `-DryRun`, does not rewrite `completed_jobs.jsonl`, and does not expose the real Backfill action in the web UI.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-read-only-failure-preview"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Read-Only Failure Preview

### Root Problem

The web Audit / Reports page showed paths to the latest failure reports but did not show the actual failure rows. Operators would still need to open raw JSON or return to the Tk Failures tab to see error codes, stages, classifications, and reasons. The existing Python failure loader already normalizes PowerShell failure records for the Tk table, so the web shell should consume that backend model instead of parsing JSON in JavaScript.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `FailurePreviewDto`.
- Added `MediaPipelineApplicationFacade.get_failure_preview()`.
- Added token-protected read-only `GET /api/failures` with `effect=none`.
- The route can read the latest round failure JSON by default or failure markers when `source=markers` is requested.
- Added a Recent Failures table to the web Audit / Reports page with classification, error code, stage, media type, title, reason, and timestamp columns.
- Added client-side failure filtering over title, path, stage, code, reason, and suggested action.
- Kept clear-failures, priority, rerun, source-open, artifact-open, and repro-open actions out of this pass.

### Validation

- Ran focused desktop API/static tests: 30 tests passed.
- Ran JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Ran full desktop test suite: 188 tests passed.
- Ran `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Ran V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Ran Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is a read-only adapter over existing failure loading logic. It does not clear failure state, create priority markers, launch reruns, open arbitrary paths, mutate reports, or touch media.

<a id="2026-05-07---release-self-test-tauri-preview-gate"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Release Self-Test Tauri Preview Gate

### Root Problem

The release self-test checked that the Tauri/WebView2 scaffold files existed, but it did not execute the existing prereq checker. That left a gap where a deployable package could pass layout checks while the scaffold/backend import contract was broken. Running the full Tauri build gate from the release self-test would be too invasive because normal release validation should not require Node.js, Rust, or MSVC on every machine.

### Files Changed

- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a `Tauri Preview Gate` section to the release self-test.
- The release self-test now runs `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1` without `-RequireToolchain` or `-RequireBuildTools`.
- Added warning passthrough for successful child checks so missing Node/Rust/MSVC remains visible without failing the standard release gate.
- Added PowerShell parser coverage for the Tauri prereq, build, and launch checker scripts.
- Added a scaffold unit test that verifies the release self-test calls the prereq checker without escalating to toolchain-required mode.

### Validation

- Ran Tauri scaffold unit test: 13 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1` without required toolchain switches: passed.
- Ran full release self-test: passed, including the new `Tauri Preview Gate`.
- Verified the persistent V5 local API on port 8765: exactly one `local_api_main` process and `/api/health` returned `ok`.

### Regression Risk

Low. The new gate is non-destructive and runs only the existing prereq checker. It can fail the release self-test for missing scaffold files or broken local API import, but it does not build the Tauri shell and does not require Node/Rust/MSVC by default.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-build-artifact-release-hygiene"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Build Artifact Release Hygiene

### Root Problem

The release builder already omitted Tauri `node_modules` and Rust `target`, but the local Tauri toolchain also generated `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\gen\schemas`. Those generated schema files are local build output and should not appear in clean deployable packages while the Tauri/WebView2 shell remains a preview scaffold.

### Files Changed

- `Build-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`
- `docs\TLDR.md`
- `docs\DEPLOYABILITY_CHECKLIST.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added release-builder exclusion for `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\gen\*`.
- Added release manifest hygiene checks that reject packaged `node_modules`, `src-tauri\gen`, or `src-tauri\target`.
- Updated reliability regression text checks so the release-builder and verifier policies stay pinned.
- Updated operator/deployability docs to state that local Tauri/WebView2 build artifacts are excluded from clean packages.

### Validation

- Ran release builder dry-run: passed. The plan excluded 4 Tauri generated schema files, 19 Tauri node module files, and 5,983 Tauri Rust build-output files.
- Ran reliability regression checks: passed.
- Built a temporary deployable copy with `-Verify -IncludeTests`: passed. The copied package manifest hygiene confirmed `node_modules`, `src-tauri\gen`, and `src-tauri\target` were absent, then the copied release self-test passed.
- Verified the persistent V5 local API on port 8765: exactly one `local_api_main` process and `/api/health` returned `ok`.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes only clean-package exclusion policy and verifier assertions. It does not remove local build artifacts from the working tree and does not affect the current Tk app, local API, or Tauri preview launcher.

<a id="2026-05-07---tv-rename-folder-season-scan-bound"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - TV Rename Folder Season Scan Bound

### Root Problem

The release self-test exposed a nondeterministic TV rename failure where a plain `E01` filename inferred `S00` instead of the user-provided/default `S01`. The folder-season resolver scanned four parent folders, so unrelated higher-level folders named like specials/extras could leak into a file that was not actually inside a season/specials folder for that show.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_rename.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_rename_service.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Limited TV folder season inference to the media file's current folder and immediate parent.
- This still supports normal layouts such as `Show\Season 2\file`, `Show\Specials\file`, and `Show\Specials\Subfolder\file`.
- Added regression coverage so a far ancestor named `Extras` does not force a plain show folder to `S00`.

### Validation

- Ran targeted rename tests: 15 tests passed.
- Ran focused application facade/local API unit tests: 38 tests passed.
- Ran full desktop Python test suite: 201 tests passed.
- Ran full release self-test: passed.
- Verified the persistent V5 local API on port 8765: exactly one `local_api_main` process and `/api/health` returned `ok`.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This intentionally narrows automatic folder-season inference to avoid root-path false positives. Deep unusual layouts where the season marker is more than one parent above the file may now default to the UI/default season and should use the left-side season field or a sidecar/folder convention closer to the media file.

- Ran full release self-test after the retry hardening: passed.

Low. This changes only the ops/release/metadata/integration test harness and does not alter production subtitle conversion logic. The hardened check still fails after two unsuccessful attempts.

<a id="2026-05-07---recursive-package-syntax-release-gate"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Recursive Package Syntax Release Gate

### Root Problem

The release self-test syntax gate still depended on a manually maintained list of Python files. As the V5 transition split the local API, application facade, contracts, payload adapters, route maps, and settings policy into smaller modules, a future extracted module could be omitted from the syntax gate and still ship with a syntax error if it was not loaded by a focused test.

### Files Changed

- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Kept the explicit critical Python syntax list for readability and continuity.
- Added recursive discovery of all project-owned `src\mediapipeline\desktop\*.py` files.
- Deduplicated the syntax-check target list before parsing.
- Left bundled runtime, third-party payloads, and non-project Python outside this recursive syntax scope.

### Validation

- Ran the full release self-test: passed.
- The release self-test recursively parsed the project package, ran the desktop Python unit suite, environment verifier, Tauri preview prerequisites, reliability regression checks, tool integration checks, and end-to-end smoke checks: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only broadens release verification and does not alter runtime behavior. The main practical risk is that future syntax errors in project files will now fail the release gate earlier, which is the intended behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-rename-preview-request-gating"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Rename Preview Request Gating

### Root Problem

The WebView rename preview button could issue overlapping `/api/rename/preview` requests. A slower stale response could repaint the preview rows after a newer request had already started, which is especially confusing for batch TV rename edits where the operator is changing season, episode, show, negative-filter, or override inputs quickly.

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a monotonic rename preview request id in the WebView rename view.
- Disabled the `Preview` button while a rename preview request is active.
- Ignored stale preview successes and failures when a newer preview request has already superseded them.
- Kept `/api/rename/preview` and `/api/rename/apply` backend behavior unchanged; this is a browser-shell state-ordering fix only.
- Added static WebView asset assertions so the local API prototype test catches accidental removal of the request-order guard.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-settings-patch-preview-stale-result-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Settings Patch Preview Stale-Result Guard

### Root Problem

The WebView settings patch preview could display a backend preview for patch JSON that the operator had already edited while the request was in flight. That creates a misleading operator state: the visible patch text and the preview details can describe different config mutations.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\settingsView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a monotonic settings patch preview request id.
- Captured the patch JSON text at request time.
- Ignored superseded preview responses.
- Marked the preview as stale when the patch JSON changes before the backend preview returns.
- Kept settings validation, save, reload, PSD1 persistence, and backend patch semantics unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_serves_read_only_web_prototype -q`: passed.
- Ran `Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed, including WebView JavaScript syntax and Rust `cargo check`.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 775 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The change only affects the WebView preview display path. It does not change the backend patch validator, save confirmation behavior, config file writes, backups, reload behavior, or the Tk settings workflow.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-maintenance-refresh-coalescing"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Maintenance Refresh Coalescing

### Root Problem

The WebView maintenance view disabled the manual refresh button during `/api/maintenance`, but command-triggered refreshes could still call `refreshMaintenance()` while a health read was already active. That allowed overlapping maintenance reads and made the final rendered health state depend on whichever response arrived last.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\maintenanceView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added an in-flight flag for maintenance refresh.
- Added a queued refresh flag so a later refresh request is not dropped; it runs once after the active request finishes.
- Kept maintenance command behavior, release dry-run behavior, completed-manifest backfill dry-run behavior, and backend contracts unchanged.
- Added static WebView asset assertions to keep the coalescing guard from being removed accidentally.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_serves_read_only_web_prototype -q`: passed.
- Ran `Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed, including WebView JavaScript syntax and Rust `cargo check`.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 775 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only serializes WebView maintenance health reads. It does not change the maintenance service, release builder dry-run, completed manifest backfill dry-run, or Tk maintenance behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-launch-command-group-gating"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Launch Command Group Gating

### Root Problem

The WebView launch shell disabled only the button that submitted a start command. Pipeline start, pending-publish drain, audit start, and CSV rerun start live on different panels, so a fast operator could submit overlapping launch commands from separate controls before the backend snapshot refreshed.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\launchView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shared launch-command button group for pipeline start, pending-publish drain, audit start, and CSV rerun start.
- Added a launch-command in-flight guard that rejects additional start submissions with an operator-visible warning while another launch request is active.
- Left pipeline control flags (`pause`, `rescan`, `stop`) separate from launch gating.
- Kept backend launch contracts, PowerShell process launch behavior, queue logic, and Tk launch behavior unchanged.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This changes WebView operator gating for launch commands, but it does not change backend launch validation or process lifecycle behavior. The main risk is temporarily blocking a legitimate second start action until the first request returns, which is safer than allowing overlapping start submissions.

<a id="2026-05-08---local-api-settings-reload-failure-diagnostics"></a>

Settings reload failures returned an error payload to the WebView, but the Local API did not leave a server-side diagnostics trail for reload exceptions. That made reload failures harder to reconstruct from logs after the UI state moved on.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-backend-http-error-body-preview"></a>

The Tauri shell reported only the HTTP status line when a backend request returned a non-200 response. During startup, contract validation, close-readiness checks, or graceful shutdown, that hid useful Local API JSON error text behind a generic `HTTP 500` or `HTTP 401` message.

Low. This changes only Tauri shell diagnostics for failed backend HTTP responses. It does not change backend routes, auth, startup contract validation, close-readiness semantics, or shutdown cleanup.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-forced-backend-termination-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Forced Backend Termination Diagnostics

### Root Problem

When the Tauri shell asked the Python backend to shut down gracefully and the backend did not exit within the grace period, the shell killed the process without clearly reporting that forced termination path. Kill/wait errors were also discarded.

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shell stderr message before forced backend termination after the graceful wait expires.
- Logged backend process kill failures.
- Logged backend process wait-after-kill failures.
- Kept graceful shutdown request behavior, the 3-second grace period, process kill fallback, and close-readiness confirmation behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 776 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is a Tauri shell diagnostics-only change around an existing forced-termination path. It does not change backend process ownership or cleanup order.

<a id="2026-05-09---facade-process-launch-lock"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Facade Process Launch Lock

### Root Problem

The WebView shell now gates launch buttons, and the existing facade active-work guard checks running processes, ActiveJobs, progress, and audit progress before starting work. However, the Local API uses a threaded HTTP server, so two near-simultaneous process-launch requests could both pass the check-then-start window before either launch was visible through process/progress state.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_guard.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_pipeline.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_audit.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_rerun.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a facade-owned nonblocking process-launch lock.
- Wrapped pipeline start, audit start, and CSV rerun start from active-work verification through service launch.
- Reused each command's existing active-work blocked result shape when another launch command is already in progress.
- Kept validation, schedule gating, launch service calls, PowerShell behavior, WebView gating, and Tk behavior otherwise unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_process_launch_commands_share_backend_launch_lock -q`: passed.
- Ran focused existing pipeline/audit/rerun launch tests: 3 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 777 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This changes backend launch-command concurrency semantics by rejecting overlapping launch requests instead of allowing them to race. That is intentional and safer for process lifecycle ownership. The lock is held only around the guard and service start call, not for the lifetime of the launched media process.

<a id="2026-05-09---facade-maintenance-command-lock"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Facade Maintenance Command Lock

### Root Problem

The WebView shell serialized release dry-run and completed-manifest backfill dry-run submissions, but the Local API can be called by multiple clients. Without a backend lock, two maintenance process-style commands could overlap and race around release-package subprocess execution, RunLogs checkpoint creation, and operator feedback.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance_command_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance_commands.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance_release.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_maintenance_backfill.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a facade-owned nonblocking maintenance-command lock.
- Wrapped release-package dry-run and completed-manifest backfill dry-run service calls with the shared lock.
- Return a standard warning `CommandResult` when another maintenance command is already active.
- Acquire the backfill lock before creating a checkpoint path, so blocked requests do not create side-effect files.
- Kept maintenance health refreshes, release builder options, backfill dry-run semantics, WebView gating, and Tk behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_maintenance_dry_run_commands_share_backend_command_lock -q`: passed.
- Ran focused existing ops/release/metadata/backfill maintenance tests: 3 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 778 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This intentionally rejects overlapping backend maintenance dry-run commands instead of allowing them to race. The lock is held only for the duration of the synchronous dry-run service call, not for unrelated maintenance health reads.

The WebView already disabled diagnostics/report, completed-row, and pending-publish open buttons while a request was active, but the functions did not keep internal in-flight state. <a id="2026-05-09---rename-apply-serialization"></a>

Rename apply is a filesystem mutation command. The WebView rejected apply while a preview was active, but it did not track an apply request already in flight. The backend also rebuilt and applied rename plans without a facade-level lock, so multiple Local API clients could attempt overlapping apply operations against the same files.

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a facade-owned nonblocking rename-apply lock.
- Held the lock from backend plan rebuild through selected-row apply so overlapping Local API apply calls cannot race the filesystem.
- Added a standard warning result for blocked backend rename apply attempts.
- Added WebView apply-in-flight state and shared button synchronization with preview-in-flight state.
- Kept preview behavior, selected-only apply behavior, rename transaction logic, sidecar handling, and Tk behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran focused rename facade/static tests: 3 tests passed.

Low-medium. This intentionally serializes rename apply operations. A legitimate second apply must wait for the first to complete, which is safer than overlapping filesystem rename transactions.

<a id="2026-05-09---settings-save-serialization"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Settings Save Serialization

### Root Problem

The WebView settings page serializes settings commands, but direct Local API clients could still submit overlapping config-save requests. Even with atomic config writes, two saves can build patches from the same stale base config and race backup/write/reload feedback.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`

- Added a facade-owned nonblocking settings-save lock.
- Held the lock from backend patch candidate construction through PSD1 serialization/save.
- Concurrent backend settings-save calls now return a warning `CommandResult` without writing config files or backups.
- Kept settings preview, validation, no-change handling, PSD1 serialization, atomic save behavior, WebView gating, and Tk behavior unchanged.

- Reran the transiently failing Local API integration test directly: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 781 tests passed.

Low-medium. This intentionally serializes backend config writes. A legitimate second save must wait for the first request to return, which is safer than overlapping patch/save operations against the same PSD1.

<a id="2026-05-09---activejobs-pid-reuse-close-readiness-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - ActiveJobs PID-Reuse Close-Readiness Hardening

### Root Problem

ActiveJobs close-readiness used PID liveness alone. If Windows reused the old PID from a stale pipeline launch record for an unrelated process, the future Tauri shell could keep warning about active MediaPipeline work even though the recorded PowerShell process was gone.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_process_active_jobs.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_service_process_active_jobs.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added an identity-aware ActiveJobs PID check for close-readiness and reconciliation.
- Kept the existing `active_job_pid_is_alive()` helper unchanged for compatibility.
- Treated a PID as stale when the process is alive but both inspected launch identity signals clearly mismatch the ActiveJobs record.
- Continued to fail closed when process identity cannot be inspected due to access denial or other psutil failures.
- Reconciled PID-reuse stale records to `orphaned` with an explicit launch-identity reason.
- Expanded the Tauri close-readiness source test to verify the close warning includes backend reason/state details.

### Validation

Low-medium. This only changes how stale ActiveJobs records are interpreted when process identity can be inspected. If identity cannot be inspected, close-readiness remains conservative and blocks close as before.

<a id="2026-05-09---backend-shutdown-callback-failure-coverage"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Backend Shutdown Callback Failure Coverage

The Local API schedules backend shutdown after returning the HTTP response to the Tauri shell. Callback failures were already logged, but the behavior lacked direct regression coverage, which made it easy to accidentally hide shutdown callback failures during future lifecycle refactors.

- Kept runtime shutdown behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran focused Local API shutdown and process spawn-runner tests: 7 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is coverage-only and documents the existing fail-visible callback behavior.

Malformed pipeline progress, audit progress, or pipeline event files were logged with generic failure text. During unattended operation, that forces the operator to hunt through the state tree to determine which runtime artifact is corrupt or partially written.

<a id="2026-05-09---command-journal-temp-cleanup-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Command Journal Temp Cleanup Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The Local API command journal uses an fsynced temporary file plus atomic replace, but cleanup failures after a failed save were silently ignored. That hid a small but real diagnostics signal when operator feedback persistence was degraded and temporary files could not be removed.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_journal.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Logged temporary command-journal cleanup failures after save failures.
- Kept command-journal save behavior, atomic replace behavior, and in-memory command history behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran focused command-journal persistence/cleanup tests: 2 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only changes the failure diagnostic path after an already-failed journal save.

<a id="2026-05-09---diagnostics-path-lookup-failure-logging"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Diagnostics Path Lookup Failure Logging

### Root Problem

Diagnostics open targets are backend allowlisted, but helper failures while locating optional targets such as latest reports were treated the same as a genuinely missing path. That hid useful error context when a report index, manifest, or state reader failed.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_diagnostics.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

- Added service-logger warnings when optional diagnostics path lookup helpers raise.
- Kept the public command result behavior unchanged: the open command still returns the existing missing-path response instead of exposing a raw exception to the frontend.

### Validation

- Ran focused diagnostics-open tests: 2 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds diagnostics only; diagnostics open allowlists and operator-facing result contracts are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-09---settings-diff-serialization-fallback-logging"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Settings Diff Serialization Fallback Logging

### Root Problem

Settings patch previews use the PSD1 serializer to show operator-friendly redacted diffs, but if that serializer failed the code silently fell back to JSON. The fallback was safe, but silent format changes make settings persistence issues harder to diagnose.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_settings_helpers.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Logged PSD1 serialization failures before falling back to redacted JSON diff text.
- Preserved the existing fallback behavior and redacted diff contract.

### Validation

- Ran focused settings diff/preview tests: 2 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes logging only; settings preview/save outputs remain compatible.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-in-flight-registry-temp-cleanup-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network In-Flight Registry Temp Cleanup Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The coordinator in-flight registry and worker crash-recovery state use same-directory temp files and atomic replace. If a save failed and the temporary file cleanup also failed, the cleanup failure was silently ignored, hiding a useful signal for state-directory permissions, locks, or disk problems.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker_state.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Logged failed temp-file cleanup after failed in-flight registry saves.
- Logged failed temp-file cleanup after failed worker crash-recovery state writes.
- Kept registry save/load contracts and existing exception-swallowing behavior unchanged.
- Kept worker-state save/load contracts and existing failure propagation unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran focused in-flight registry and worker-state cleanup tests: 4 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 82 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes only diagnostics after already-failed network state saves.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-poll-startup-and-crash-state-cleanup-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Poll Startup And Crash-State Cleanup Hardening

### Root Problem

Network worker startup parsed `WorkerPollIntervalSecs` with a direct `int(...)` conversion. A malformed setting could crash worker startup before the operator received a useful status. Separately, failure to clear `worker_state.json` after a successful coordinator state transition was logged only at debug level, even though stale worker state can trigger duplicate crash-recovery reports on restart.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\poll_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_worker_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added `resolve_worker_poll_interval()` to coerce malformed poll interval settings to the documented default and clamp too-small values to 5 seconds.
- Logged invalid or too-small worker poll interval settings so fallback behavior is visible.
- Updated `WorkerDispatcher` startup to use the poll policy helper instead of direct integer parsing.
- Promoted failed worker crash-state cleanup from debug to warning.
- Added behavior and source-policy coverage for the poll interval helper and worker usage.
- Added coverage for operator-visible worker-state cleanup failures.

### Validation

- Ran focused poll/worker-state tests: 7 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 88 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Malformed worker poll intervals now fall back instead of crashing. Valid values keep the existing minimum 5-second clamp.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-view-background-ui-handoff-diagnostics"></a>

Several Network tab background operations scheduled Tk updates directly from worker threads without guarding `root.after(...)`. If the UI was closing or disrupted, those background failures could vanish while buttons remained disabled or operator status stayed stale.

- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Logged UI handoff failures for coordinator URL tests.
- Logged UI handoff failures for coordinator mDNS discovery result display.
- Logged UI handoff failures for Windows Firewall check and add-rule results.
- Kept UI behavior and network/firewall probing behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 92 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds diagnostics around Tk callback scheduling only.

mDNS coordinator discovery logged ServiceBrowser setup failures, but Zeroconf close failures during discovery cleanup were swallowed. On Windows workstations with optional Bonjour/Zeroconf support, that could hide local discovery cleanup problems while the Network tab simply reported no coordinator found.

- Added a fake Zeroconf/ServiceBrowser test that verifies discovery still returns an empty result while preserving the cleanup failure in logs.
- Kept mDNS discovery behavior, optional dependency behavior, and Network tab behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 95 tests passed.

Low. This changes only logging after discovery cleanup failures.

<a id="2026-05-09---cluster-log-rotation-failure-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Cluster Log Rotation Failure Visibility

### Root Problem

Coordinator cluster-log rotation is intentionally best-effort, but rotation failures were swallowed completely. If `cluster.log.1` was locked, malformed, or replaced by a directory on a Windows share, the active `cluster.log` could keep growing past the intended cap without leaving an operator-visible diagnostic.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a warning when cluster-log rotation fails, while still appending the new log entry.
- Added a focused test that forces the backup path to fail rotation and verifies append continuity plus the warning.
- Kept cluster-log append, event formatting, and network protocol behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.WorkflowEnhancementTests.test_cluster_log_rotation_failure_is_logged_without_blocking_append DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 87 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only surfaces a failed best-effort rotation path that was already being ignored.

<a id="2026-05-09---shared-local-ip-selection-policy"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Shared Local IP Selection Policy

### Root Problem

The Network tab and mDNS advertiser each carried their own copy of LAN IPv4 selection logic. That created a drift risk where the operator-facing share block could recommend one address while mDNS advertised another, especially on machines with VPN adapters, link-local addresses, or multiple private subnets.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\local_ip.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\mdns.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\views\network_tab.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_local_ip.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shared local-IP policy helper for RFC-1918 priority, hostname IPv4 filtering, and UDP-route fallback.
- Updated mDNS advertising to use the shared primary-IP policy while preserving its `127.0.0.1` last-resort fallback.
- Updated the Network tab to use the same shared policy while preserving its empty-string UI fallback.
- Added tests for LAN/VPN priority order, invalid/loopback/link-local filtering, and UDP fallback failure behavior.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_local_ip DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 99 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The intended selection order is unchanged, but Network tab display and mDNS advertising now share the same implementation. Machines with unusual adapter ordering should be watched once in the UI after update.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-tab-live-hot-swap-failure-logging"></a>

Two Network tab convenience actions caught live WorkerDispatcher hot-swap failures without logging: applying a discovered coordinator URL and pasting a worker auth token. The UI fell back to generic success-ish text, which made it hard to tell whether the value was only placed in the field or actually applied to a running worker.

Low. This adds diagnostics only; the UI still updates fields and keeps the prior fallback status behavior.

The Network tab could show dialogs when opening RunLogs or `cluster.log` failed, but those failures were not written to logs. That left the future diagnostics/support path weaker because the operator had to remember a transient dialog instead of having a durable error trail.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-controller-lifecycle-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network Controller Lifecycle Diagnostics

### Root Problem

`NetworkController` still suppressed dispatcher shutdown failures during role replacement/start/stop paths, and worker poll-status UI posting failures could disappear inside the callback. Those failures matter because they can leave stale worker/coordinator threads alive or leave the Network tab showing stale status.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\network_controller.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_controllers.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

- Logged worker poll-status UI post failures from the worker callback.
- Added controller tests for dispatcher shutdown diagnostics and worker-status post diagnostics.

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_controllers.ControllerBoundaryTests.test_network_controller_logs_dispatcher_shutdown_failure DesktopApp.tests.test_controllers.ControllerBoundaryTests.test_network_controller_logs_worker_status_post_failure DesktopApp.tests.test_controllers -q`: 88 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Dispatcher replacement and fallback behavior is unchanged; failures that were previously suppressed are now logged.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-heartbeat-progress-contract-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network Heartbeat Progress Contract Hardening

### Root Problem

Worker heartbeat progress was parsed with a raw `float(...)` and stored directly in coordinator state. A malformed or buggy worker could send NaN, Infinity, negative, or greater-than-100 progress values, which could corrupt persisted in-flight state and produce misleading worker-board rendering.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\protocol.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shared `coerce_progress_percent()` protocol helper.
- Rejected non-finite heartbeat progress values.
- Clamped valid heartbeat progress into the 0..100 range before it reaches `HeartbeatRequest` or `InFlightRegistry`.
- Added tests for heartbeat DTO coercion and registry heartbeat clamping.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.NetworkPersistenceTests.test_heartbeat_request_progress_is_finite_and_clamped DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.NetworkPersistenceTests.test_inflight_registry_heartbeat_clamps_progress_percent DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 90 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. Normal worker progress is unchanged. Out-of-contract workers now receive invalid-body handling for non-finite progress, and out-of-range finite progress is clamped instead of displayed/persisted raw.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-state-strict-json-persistence"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network State Strict JSON Persistence

### Root Problem

Python `json.dumps()` writes `NaN` and `Infinity` by default, which is not strict JSON. Network in-flight state and worker crash-recovery state could therefore persist invalid JSON if any non-finite value reached those payloads, making external diagnostics and stricter parsers unreliable.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker_state.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Set `allow_nan=False` for coordinator in-flight registry saves.
- Set `allow_nan=False` for worker crash-recovery state saves.
- Added coverage proving non-finite registry state is rejected without writing an invalid state file.
- Added coverage proving non-finite pending done-report state is rejected before writing `worker_state.json`.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.NetworkPersistenceTests.test_worker_state_rejects_nonfinite_pending_done_json DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.NetworkPersistenceTests.test_inflight_registry_save_rejects_nonfinite_json_state DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 92 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. Valid JSON state is unchanged. If a future path attempts to persist non-finite values, it now fails visibly instead of writing invalid JSON.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-http-strict-json-payloads"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network HTTP Strict JSON Payloads

### Root Problem

The worker/coordinator HTTP JSON helper also used default `json.dumps()` behavior, so non-finite values could be sent over the local network as `NaN` or `Infinity`. That made the wire contract less strict and pushed malformed payload detection to the receiving endpoint.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\http_json.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Set `allow_nan=False` for outbound HTTP JSON POST bodies.
- Added coverage proving non-finite outbound payloads fail before `urlopen()` is called.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.NetworkPersistenceTests.test_http_json_post_rejects_nonfinite_payload_before_request DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 92 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. Valid requests are unchanged. Malformed non-finite payloads now fail on the sender before any network call is attempted.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-strict-json-response-fallback"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Strict JSON Response Fallback

### Root Problem

Coordinator response serialization also used permissive `json.dumps()` behavior. If a bad value reached a response payload, the request handler could emit non-strict JSON or crash while trying to respond, leaving worker/UI callers with poor diagnostics.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Set `allow_nan=False` for coordinator JSON responses.
- Applied the same strict JSON encoder to the oversized-request `413` response path.
- Added a safe fallback that logs the serialization failure and returns a strict `500` JSON envelope.
- Added coverage for non-finite response payload fallback behavior.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence.NetworkPersistenceTests.test_coordinator_send_json_rejects_nonfinite_response_payload DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 93 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Valid responses are unchanged. Invalid response payloads now fail into a strict JSON error instead of emitting permissive JSON or crashing the handler.

<a id="2026-05-09---local-api-strict-json-response-fallback"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Local API Strict JSON Response Fallback

### Root Problem

The Local API used by the Tauri/WebView shell also serialized responses with permissive JSON behavior. If a backend route returned NaN or Infinity, the WebView could receive non-standard JSON or the handler could fail without a clean API envelope.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\handler.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Set `allow_nan=False` for Local API JSON responses.
- Added a logged strict `500` fallback envelope for non-strict Local API response payloads.
- Ensured failed response serialization does not record a successful command journal entry.
- Added handler-level coverage for non-finite Local API response payloads.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_send_json_rejects_nonfinite_response_payload DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade -q`: 66 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Valid Local API responses are unchanged. Invalid route payloads now become explicit JSON errors instead of non-standard JSON.

<a id="2026-05-09---local-api-command-journal-strict-json-writes"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Local API Command Journal Strict JSON Writes

### Root Problem

The Local API command journal persisted operator command summaries with permissive JSON settings. Normal summaries are sanitized, but corrupted in-memory history could still leak `NaN`/`Infinity` or leave temp files behind if serialization failed during the atomic write.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_journal.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Set `allow_nan=False` for command-journal saves.
- Extended save failure handling to catch JSON serialization errors as well as OS errors.
- Preserved temp-file cleanup and warning behavior for failed saves.
- Added coverage for non-finite command-journal state cleanup.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_command_journal_rejects_nonfinite_json_and_cleans_temp_file DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade -q`: 67 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid command summaries are unchanged. Corrupted non-finite command history now fails visibly without leaving temp files behind.

<a id="2026-05-09---local-api-strict-json-request-bodies"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Local API Strict JSON Request Bodies

### Root Problem

Python `json.loads()` accepts `NaN`, `Infinity`, and `-Infinity` constants by default. The Local API request parser could therefore pass non-standard JSON values from the WebView command layer into backend route handlers.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\http_helpers.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a Local API JSON parse-constant rejection hook.
- Returned the existing `400 invalid json body` envelope for non-finite JSON constants.
- Added coverage for rejecting a request body containing `NaN`.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade.ApplicationFacadeTests.test_local_api_json_body_rejects_nonfinite_constants DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade -q`: 68 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Valid request JSON is unchanged. Non-standard JSON constants are now rejected before route handlers see them.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-strict-json-reads"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network Strict JSON Reads

### Root Problem

Network-mode reads still used default `json.loads()` in several places, so `NaN`/`Infinity` could enter from coordinator HTTP responses, worker/coordinator request bodies, `worker_state.json`, or JSON-backed network settings even though writes had already been hardened.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\json_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\http_json.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker_state.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\path_map.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\encode_config_snapshot.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shared strict JSON read helper for network code.
- Rejected non-finite constants in worker HTTP response parsing.
- Rejected non-finite constants in coordinator `/api/done`, `/api/heartbeat`, and `/api/log` request bodies.
- Rejected non-finite constants when loading worker crash-recovery state and in-flight registry state.
- Rejected non-finite constants in `WorkerSourcePathMap` and `WorkerConfigOverrides` JSON settings.
- Added focused coverage for HTTP response, coordinator request body, worker state, and network config JSON rejection.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-shutdown-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Shutdown Diagnostics

### Root Problem

Coordinator shutdown still used broad exception suppression for HTTP server teardown, mDNS advertiser cleanup, and final in-flight registry persistence. A failed shutdown, stuck reaper, or failed final state flush could leave network-mode recovery state ambiguous with no operator-visible evidence.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Replaced silent coordinator shutdown suppressions with warning logs for HTTP shutdown, HTTP close, mDNS stop, reaper join, and final registry save failures.
- Added a bounded 2-second stale-reaper join during shutdown so normal exits are cleaner without hanging indefinitely.
- Preserved existing shutdown behavior by still clearing server/advertiser references and continuing teardown after individual component failures.
- Added focused coverage for multiple teardown failures and a stuck reaper.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_local_ip DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 112 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 817 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal coordinator shutdown is unchanged except for a bounded reaper join. Failure cases now produce diagnostics instead of disappearing.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-controller-ui-diagnostics"></a>

The Network controller still had broad suppression around several UI-state updates. If dispatcher fallback status, coordinator button refresh, or the home coordinator notice failed, the controller could continue with state changes while leaving the operator-facing UI stale without any diagnostic trail.

- Replaced the remaining Network-controller `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` paths with targeted warning logs.

- Added coverage for coordinator notice failures, coordinator button update failures, and dispatcher error status update failures.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-cluster-log-dispatch-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Cluster Log Dispatch Guard

### Root Problem

Worker cluster-log events are intentionally best-effort and already mirror to the local Python log, but the fire-and-forget POST thread startup itself was not guarded. If thread creation/start failed, a convenience diagnostics path could interrupt worker control flow.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Wrapped async cluster-log POST thread startup in a warning-logged `try/except`.
- Preserved local log mirroring and existing best-effort HTTP POST behavior.
- Added coverage that simulates thread start failure and verifies no HTTP POST is attempted.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 98 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_local_ip DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 113 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 821 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Successful cluster-log dispatch is unchanged. Only failure to start the background diagnostics thread now becomes visible and non-disruptive.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-heartbeat-progress-sanitization"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Heartbeat Progress Sanitization

### Root Problem

Worker heartbeat payloads read progress directly from the local snapshot. A malformed or non-finite `CurrentStagePercent` value could trip strict JSON serialization on the worker and be reported as a heartbeat/network failure even though the coordinator and network path were healthy.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Reused the shared heartbeat progress coercion helper at the worker send boundary.
- Clamped out-of-range finite progress values to `0..100`.
- Treated malformed/non-finite snapshot progress as `0.0` while preserving the current stage label.
- Added focused coverage for `NaN`, over-100, and negative snapshot progress values.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 99 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_local_ip DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 114 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 822 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid heartbeat progress is unchanged. Bad local telemetry now degrades to a bounded progress value instead of causing a false heartbeat POST failure.

<a id="2026-05-09---network-protocol-numeric-contract-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Network Protocol Numeric Contract Hardening

### Root Problem

Strict JSON parsing rejects literal `NaN` and `Infinity`, but JSON strings such as `"NaN"` could still be coerced by protocol DTOs into non-finite floats. That left claim size, done timing, heartbeat FPS, and retry timing fields vulnerable to invalid numeric values after the JSON boundary.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\protocol.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added shared finite-float and non-negative integer coercion helpers for network protocol DTOs.
- Rejected non-finite claim `estimated_size_gb`, done `elapsed_seconds`, and heartbeat `fps` values.
- Rejected negative retry-after, output-size, and ETA values.
- Added focused protocol coverage for non-finite numeric strings and negative values.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 100 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_local_ip DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 115 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 823 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Valid numeric payloads are unchanged. Invalid payloads that previously slipped through as strings now fail at the DTO boundary and are handled as invalid request/response bodies.

When the coordinator could not read the in-flight registry active count, it silently fell back to the idle retry hint. That preserved worker behavior but hid a possible registry/state bug that affects cluster polling cadence.

<a id="2026-05-09---in-flight-registry-numeric-state-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - In-Flight Registry Numeric State Hardening

### Root Problem

Coordinator in-flight state had strict JSON reads/writes, but numeric strings inside persisted state could still coerce to non-finite floats during crash recovery. Invalid `estimated_size_gb`, worker throughput GB, or worker timing values could therefore re-enter memory and later break strict persistence or distort the worker board.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Reused shared finite-float and non-negative integer coercion helpers inside in-flight state load paths.
- Skipped malformed persisted jobs and worker stats that contain non-finite or negative numeric fields.
- Sanitized non-finite claim-time `estimated_size_gb` to `0.0` with a warning before the job reaches persisted state.
- Extended existing registry persistence tests with bad-size job and bad-GB worker-stat cases.

### Validation

- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Valid state is unchanged. Corrupt persisted numeric state is now skipped or sanitized rather than rehydrated.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-claim-size-estimate-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Claim Size Estimate Guard

### Root Problem

The coordinator HTTP and local claim paths converted `record.estimated_size_gb` with `float(...)` before calling the registry. A malformed queue record such as `"not-a-number"` could therefore raise inside the claim handler before the registry sanitizer could fallback to a safe estimate.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a coordinator-level queue-record size coercion helper.
- Reused the shared finite-float/non-negative contract helper.
- Warned and fell back to `0.0` when a queue record carries malformed or non-finite estimated size.
- Added HTTP claim coverage that verifies malformed size estimates no longer crash the claim path.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 102 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_local_ip DesktopApp.tests.test_network_mdns DesktopApp.tests.test_network_view_source_policy DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 117 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 825 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid size estimates are unchanged. Invalid estimates now produce a warning and a safe zero estimate instead of interrupting claim handling.

<a id="2026-05-09---process-lifecycle-shutdown-cleanup-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Process Lifecycle Shutdown Cleanup Diagnostics

### Root Problem

Desktop shutdown still silently suppressed cleanup failures for taskbar progress reset, Tk poll callback cancellation, and `root.quit()`. Those failures are not usually fatal, but hiding them makes Tauri/WebView close-readiness and legacy Tk shutdown issues harder to diagnose.

### Implementation

- Replaced shutdown cleanup suppressions with best-effort warning logs.
- Preserved shutdown behavior: cleanup continues after taskbar, poll-cancel, or `root.quit()` failures, and `root.destroy()` still runs.
- Added a fallback logger helper so shutdown diagnostics still route somewhere if the service logger itself is unavailable.
- Added controller coverage for taskbar reset, poll cancellation, and root quit failures.

### Regression Risk

Low. Successful shutdown is unchanged. Non-critical cleanup failures now produce diagnostics instead of being hidden.

<a id="2026-05-09---status-server-strict-json-telemetry"></a>

The legacy status server encoded snapshot/status payloads with permissive `json.dumps()`. If telemetry contained `NaN` or `Infinity`, the endpoint could emit non-standard JSON even though the newer Local API had already been made strict.

- Added controller coverage for nested `NaN`, `Infinity`, and `-Infinity` telemetry values.

The app-state controller silently ignored failures while reading the active Library tab and restoring saved tree column widths. That made persisted UI-state drift difficult to diagnose after startup or shutdown.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\app_state_controller.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_controllers.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added warning logging when the Library tab cannot be read for persisted app state.
- Added warning logging when a saved tree column width cannot be restored.
- Preserved existing persistence behavior: unreadable optional UI state is skipped, and invalid/small saved widths are ignored.
- Added controller coverage for both diagnostic paths.

### Validation

- Ran focused app-state diagnostics test: 1 test passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 838 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Optional UI-state failures remain non-fatal. The difference is that startup/shutdown persistence drift now leaves warnings.

<a id="2026-05-09---source-path-map-entry-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Source Path-Map Entry Diagnostics

### Root Problem

WorkerSourcePathMap parsing warned on invalid JSON, but silently skipped individual mapping entries when key or value normalization failed. A single bad entry could therefore disappear while later worker path rewrites still loaded, leaving the operator without enough evidence to diagnose why one source root was not being rewritten.

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added warning logging for unparseable individual WorkerSourcePathMap entries.
- Added a safe representation helper so even broken `repr()` implementations cannot break diagnostic logging.
- Preserved existing behavior: malformed entries are skipped and later valid entries still load.
- Added network persistence coverage for malformed key/value conversion with a valid mapping after the malformed entries.

### Validation

- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 104 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 850 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Successful path rewrites are unchanged. Only previously silent malformed mapping-entry skips now emit warnings.

<a id="2026-05-09---retry-hint-policy-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Retry-Hint Policy Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The shared coordinator retry-hint policy silently fell back to the idle poll interval when its active-count input could not be coerced. That made malformed helper inputs indistinguishable from a truly idle cluster. The coordinator registry-failure path already logged its own warning, so the policy hardening also needed to avoid duplicate warning cascades there.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added warning logging for invalid `active_count` inputs to `compute_retry_after_seconds()`.
- Preserved fallback behavior: invalid inputs still return the idle retry interval.
- Updated `CoordinatorDispatcher._compute_retry_after_seconds()` to return the idle hint immediately after its existing registry-failure warning, avoiding duplicate diagnostics.
- Updated existing coordinator policy coverage to assert the warning path.

### Validation

- Ran focused coordinator policy retry-hint test: 1 test passed.
- Ran focused coordinator registry retry-hint diagnostics test: 1 test passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 851 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Retry interval values are unchanged. Invalid helper inputs now leave a warning, and coordinator registry failures still emit the existing single coordinator warning.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-request-identifier-diagnostics"></a>

Coordinator request handlers rejected parsed-but-invalid worker/job identifiers and missing cluster-log events with the correct 400 response, but those contract violations did not leave coordinator-side diagnostics. That made it harder to distinguish auth/network issues from worker payload construction bugs.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-cluster-log-field-sanitization-diagnostics"></a>

Worker-emitted `/api/log` entries are sanitized before append so invalid identifiers and oversized fields cannot corrupt `cluster.log`. That was safe, but silent; stripped worker/job IDs and truncated event/message fields could make cluster logs harder to interpret without any indication that sanitation occurred.

- Added warning logging when `/api/log` strips invalid job IDs.

- Preserved existing sanitized append behavior and HTTP response shape.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-heartbeat-cleanup-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Heartbeat Cleanup Diagnostics

### Root Problem

Worker ops/release/metadata/shutdown stops the per-job heartbeat thread with a bounded join. If that thread did not exit within the timeout, the worker dropped its reference silently, hiding a possible stuck heartbeat loop during shutdown or job release.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added warning logging when a worker heartbeat thread remains alive after the existing 5-second join.
- Preserved the existing cleanup behavior and timeout.
- Added focused network persistence coverage for the stuck-thread warning.

### Validation

- Ran focused worker heartbeat cleanup diagnostics test: 1 test passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 861 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Heartbeat cleanup timing and state cleanup are unchanged. Stuck heartbeat threads now leave warning-level diagnostics.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-heartbeat-start-recovery"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Heartbeat-Start Recovery

### Root Problem

After a worker claimed a job, `_on_job_claimed()` saved worker crash state and then started the heartbeat thread. If the heartbeat thread failed to start, the exception could escape the poll loop after the job was already marked active, leaving the coordinator to recover only after stale-job timeout.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Wrapped worker heartbeat thread start with explicit error handling.
- On heartbeat-start failure, the worker now logs the failure, updates operator status, emits a cluster-log event, and releases the claimed job immediately.
- Preserved the normal successful claim path.

### Validation

- Ran focused worker heartbeat-start recovery test: 1 test passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 862 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal heartbeat startup is unchanged. The only behavior change is the failure path: a job whose heartbeat cannot start is released immediately instead of waiting for coordinator timeout.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-claim-handoff-recovery"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Claim-Handoff Recovery

### Root Problem

The worker poll loop marked a claimed job active and then delegated post-claim setup to `_on_job_claimed()`. Unexpected exceptions during that handoff could terminate the poll loop while the job remained active locally and in-flight on the coordinator, delaying recovery until stale-job timeout.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Wrapped post-claim handoff in an explicit recovery guard.
- On unexpected handoff failure, the worker now logs the exception, updates operator status, and releases the claim immediately.
- Preserved normal successful claim behavior.

### Validation

- Ran focused worker claim-handoff recovery test: 1 test passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 863 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal worker claim execution is unchanged. Unexpected post-claim failures now release the job immediately instead of waiting for coordinator stale-timeout recovery.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-claim-preparation-recovery"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Claim-Preparation Recovery

### Root Problem

The worker poll loop could receive a successful `/api/claim` response, then fail before a local `ClaimedJob` was created. Malformed claimed response bodies and source path-map exceptions were both capable of leaving the coordinator-side claim active until stale-heartbeat recovery.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added identity-only release handling for claimed jobs whose full response body cannot be trusted.
- Added malformed claimed-response recovery that logs, emits a `claim_response_invalid` cluster event when possible, and releases the job immediately.
- Added source path-map failure handling that logs, emits a `path_map_failed` cluster event, and releases the job immediately.
- Preserved normal claim, path-map, record-build, and handoff behavior.

### Validation

- Ran focused worker claim-preparation recovery tests: 3 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 867 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal worker claim execution is unchanged. Failure paths before local job creation now release coordinator claims immediately instead of relying on stale-timeout recovery.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-poll-thread-shutdown-diagnostics"></a>

### Root Problem

Worker shutdown joined the background poll thread with a bounded timeout, but if the thread remained alive after that timeout the shutdown path continued silently. That made stuck worker poll loops hard to distinguish from clean shutdowns during unattended operation.

- Added focused network persistence coverage for the stuck poll-thread warning.

- Wrapped local coordinator release in-flight save failures with warning diagnostics while preserving the release mutation.
- Wrapped local coordinator heartbeat failures with warning diagnostics and a keep-alive result so malformed telemetry does not abort the active job.

The Tk worker-board controller trusted the dispatcher snapshot and each worker row render. A snapshot exception could abort the board refresh, and a single bad row or tree insertion failure could prevent later valid workers from rendering.
