<a id="2026-05-09---worker-lifecycle-controller-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Lifecycle Controller Guardrails

### Root Problem

The Tk worker controller still had several UI-boundary failure modes after the worker-board hardening pass. A malformed completion-event object could crash completion reporting after an encode finished, unexpected completion submit failures could leave stale active-job state in the UI, post-launch status-rendering failures could misreport a running worker encode as failed, and reclaimed-job abort could fail before killing the already-running process tree if Tk status updates or the UI background queue were unavailable.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\worker_controller.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_controllers.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Malformed completion-event rows are now skipped with warning diagnostics.
- Completion report build failures now fail closed as non-terminal worker failures instead of crashing the controller.
- Unexpected dispatcher completion-submit failures now log, surface operator status when possible, and clear stale UI active-job state.
- Worker process launch failures remain terminal report attempts, but dispatcher reporting crashes no longer bubble out of the controller.
- Worker process launch is now separated from post-launch UI/status bookkeeping so status-widget failures do not falsely report a running encode as failed.
- Reclaimed-job abort status updates are best-effort, and failure to schedule the background process-tree kill now falls back to a synchronous kill attempt before returning the worker to idle.

### Validation

- Ran focused worker lifecycle controller selections: 14 test executions passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 886 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 144 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal worker start, completion, and reclaim behavior is unchanged. The changed paths are exceptional controller-boundary failures; the main risk is that an unexpected dispatcher completion crash now clears Tk active-job state after logging, which favors avoiding a permanently stuck worker UI over preserving a non-retryable in-memory reference.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-claimdoneheartbeat-state-failure-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Claim/Done/Heartbeat State-Failure Guardrails

### Root Problem

Coordinator HTTP handlers validated request bodies and identifiers, but some registry/state operations inside `/api/claim`, `/api/done`, and `/api/heartbeat` were still allowed to unwind the handler. In network mode, an unstructured coordinator handler failure makes worker behavior harder to diagnose and can be worse after `/api/claim` has already created an in-flight job. A post-claim retry-policy exception could leave the job claimed even though the worker never received the claim response.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Wrapped `/api/claim` scan/registry claim failures and return logged `{"error": "claim unavailable"}` HTTP 500 responses.
- If retry-policy lookup fails after a successful claim, the coordinator now immediately unclaims the job before returning the same structured 500 response.
- Wrapped `/api/done` completion and release registry failures and return logged `{"error": "done state unavailable"}` HTTP 500 responses.
- Wrapped `/api/heartbeat` registry failures and return logged `{"error": "heartbeat unavailable"}` HTTP 500 responses.
- Normal successful, empty, forbidden, and not-found coordinator HTTP behavior is unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran focused coordinator state-failure tests: 5 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 149 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 891 tests passed.
- Ran `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1 -SkipLinkCheck`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. This changes only exceptional registry/state failure paths. The post-claim retry-policy failure path now actively releases the claim, which is safer than waiting for stale-heartbeat recovery but does change that rare failure from delayed reclaim to immediate availability.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-cluster-log-control-flow-isolation"></a>

Worker cluster-log events are meant to be diagnostics, but several lifecycle paths still called them directly before required control-flow steps. If cluster logging raised during path remap, claim-record failure handling, job handoff, heartbeat reclaim handling, completion reporting, or clean release reporting, a best-effort diagnostic path could block claim release, prevent a valid claimed job from being handed to the app, or interrupt worker bookkeeping.

- Converted lifecycle-sensitive worker cluster-log calls to use the safe wrapper.
- Preserved existing local warning diagnostics when cluster logging fails.
- Covered claim-record failure and path-remap paths to prove cluster-log failures do not block claim release or valid job handoff.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-shutdown-active-count-guardrail"></a>

Coordinator shutdown read `InFlightRegistry.active_count` only to include the active-job count in a cluster-log message before stopping the reaper, HTTP server, and mDNS advertiser. If that diagnostic count lookup raised, shutdown could abort before performing the actual teardown.

- Teardown continues through reaper stop, HTTP server close, mDNS stop, and final in-flight registry save.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-http-startup-cleanup-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator HTTP Startup Cleanup Guardrail

### Root Problem

Coordinator HTTP startup assigned `_http_server` before the serving thread had successfully started, and only the bind failure path had explicit diagnostics. If thread startup failed after the server object was created, the coordinator could keep a reference to a non-serving server and report a weaker startup failure.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `_http_server` is now assigned only after the serving thread starts successfully.
- Startup failures after server construction now close the partially-created server best-effort.
- Startup failures clear `_http_server`, log the failure, and still raise `RuntimeError` so network mode fails closed.
- Existing bind-failure behavior is preserved.

### Validation

- Ran focused coordinator HTTP startup failure tests: 2 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 143 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 160 tests passed.
- Ran focused transient Local API shutdown test after one full-suite connection-aborted error: 1 test passed.
- Reran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 901 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal coordinator startup is unchanged. The changed path is startup failure after socket bind but before the serve thread is running; it now cleans up and fails closed.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-reaper-startup-cleanup-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Reaper Startup Cleanup Guardrail

### Root Problem

Coordinator startup begins serving HTTP before starting the stale-job reaper. If the reaper thread failed to start, constructor failure could leave a partially initialized coordinator still listening for workers.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `_start_reaper()` now clears `_reaper_thread`, logs, and raises `RuntimeError` if the stale-job reaper thread cannot start.
- `CoordinatorDispatcher.__init__()` now tears down a partially-started coordinator if a later startup step raises after HTTP startup; coverage exercises the stale-reaper thread-start failure path.
- The cleanup path reuses `shutdown()` so accepting-claims state, HTTP server close, mDNS stop, reaper state, cluster logging, and in-flight registry persistence follow the normal teardown contract.

### Validation

- Ran focused coordinator reaper startup cleanup tests: 2 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 145 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 162 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 903 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Normal coordinator startup is unchanged. The exceptional path after HTTP startup now cleans up and fails closed instead of leaving a partial listener active.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-poll-thread-startup-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Poll-Thread Startup Guardrail

### Root Problem

Worker startup created the background poll thread and then started it without a guarded failure path. If the thread could not start, the constructor failed with a low-context thread exception and left the partially initialized dispatcher holding a stale poll-thread reference.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Wrapped the worker poll-thread `start()` call during `WorkerDispatcher` construction.
- On startup failure, the dispatcher now clears `_poll_thread`, logs the coordinator URL and failure reason, and raises a clear `RuntimeError`.
- Successful worker startup, crash recovery, cluster-log startup diagnostics, and poll-loop behavior are unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran focused worker poll-thread startup guardrail test: 1 test passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 146 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 163 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 904 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Normal worker startup still starts the same daemon poll thread. The changed path is only a thread-start failure, which now fails closed with useful diagnostics.

mDNS is optional, but startup failures still affect operator setup because workers may rely on discovery. Coordinator advertisement startup failures were debug-only, and async discovery thread-start failures surfaced as low-context thread errors unless every caller wrapped them perfectly.

- Async mDNS discovery thread-start failures now log a warning and raise a clear `RuntimeError`.
- Existing successful advertisement, discovery, cleanup, optional dependency, and Network tab discovery behavior are preserved.

<a id="2026-05-09---mdns-registration-failure-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - mDNS Registration Failure Cleanup

### Root Problem

Coordinator mDNS registration failures were logged inside `CoordinatorAdvertiser.start()`, but `start()` returned normally. `CoordinatorDispatcher` could keep the advertiser in `_mdns` even though service registration failed, making shutdown and lifecycle state look active.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\mdns.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_mdns.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `CoordinatorAdvertiser.start()` now returns `True` only after registration succeeds.
- Registration failures close any partial Zeroconf instance, clear `_zc` and `_info`, log cleanup failures if needed, and return `False`.
- `CoordinatorDispatcher._start_mdns()` stores `_mdns` only when advertiser startup succeeds; otherwise it warns with manual coordinator URL fallback guidance.

### Validation

### Regression Risk

Low. Successful mDNS advertisement behavior is unchanged. Failed registration now leaves coordinator state honest and keeps the existing manual URL fallback path visible.

<a id="2026-05-09---worker-accepted-report-cleanup-separation"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Worker Accepted-Report Cleanup Separation

### Root Problem

Worker done/release methods grouped the coordinator `/api/done` POST and local `worker_state.json` cleanup in the same failure path. The normal cleanup helper catches filesystem cleanup failures, but the control flow still made it too easy for a future cleanup exception to be treated like a failed coordinator report and persisted as a pending done retry.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_worker_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added an accepted-report cleanup helper that treats local state cleanup as best-effort after the coordinator accepts a done/release report.
- `mark_done()`, `release()`, and internal `_do_release()` now save pending done retry payloads only when the coordinator POST fails.
- Cleanup failures after accepted reports warn explicitly that no pending done report is being saved.

### Validation

- Ran focused accepted-report cleanup tests and worker source-policy test: 4 tests passed.
- Ran Python syntax checks for touched worker/test files.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 155 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_worker_source_policy -q`: 4 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 177 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 918 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Coordinator POST payloads and success/failure semantics are unchanged; this only separates accepted-report cleanup from retry persistence.

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

- Pending done recovery cleanup now uses the accepted-report cleanup helper.
- Crash-failed recovery cleanup now uses the accepted-report cleanup helper.
- Cleanup failures after accepted crash-recovery reports warn with job context instead of bubbling out of worker startup.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-cluster-log-timestamp-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Cluster-Log Timestamp Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The coordinator `/api/log` handler rewrites each cluster-log entry timestamp to the coordinator receive time and preserves the worker-supplied timestamp as a side-channel tag. If that side-channel preservation failed, the handler silently fell back to coordinator time only, leaving no clue that worker clock-skew metadata had been dropped.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_coordinator_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

- `/api/log` now warns if it cannot preserve the original worker timestamp side-channel.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-oversized-request-response-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Oversized-Request Response Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The coordinator rejects oversized request bodies before reading them, but if sending the 413 response failed because the client socket was already broken, the failure was only logged at DEBUG level. Normal operator logs could therefore show no explanation for why the oversized rejection response never reached the client.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_coordinator_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Failed attempts to send the oversized-request 413 response now warn with the exception text.
- The log message explicitly notes that the client may not receive the 413 response.
- The request-body path still returns `None` and avoids reading the oversized body.
- Runtime and source-policy tests cover the warning-level diagnostic.

### Validation

- Ran focused oversized-request response diagnostic tests: 3 tests passed.
- Ran Python syntax checks for touched coordinator/test files.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 163 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_coordinator_source_policy -q`: 3 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 190 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 931 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Oversized-body rejection behavior and response shape are unchanged; this only raises a failed 413 send attempt from DEBUG to warning.

<a id="2026-05-09---coordinator-body-read-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-09 - Coordinator Body-Read Diagnostics

### Root Problem

After the coordinator accepted a bounded `Content-Length`, a socket failure while reading the request body could escape without coordinator-specific context. The request could not be dispatched safely because there was no usable body, but the operator log did not explain why dispatch stopped.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_coordinator_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Accepted bounded request bodies now wrap the socket `read()` call.
- Read failures now warn with the accepted content length and exception text.
- The body helper returns `None` after a read failure so endpoint dispatch stops instead of processing a missing body.
- Runtime and source-policy tests cover the warning-level diagnostic.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-10---stale-reclaim-cluster-log-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Stale-Reclaim Cluster-Log Guardrail

### Root Problem

The stale-job reaper logged each reclaimed job to the coordinator cluster log before saving the updated in-flight registry. If that diagnostic cluster-log call unexpectedly raised, the broad reaper guard could skip the save for reclaimed state.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

<a id="2026-05-10---stale-report-cluster-log-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Stale-Report Cluster-Log Guardrails

### Root Problem

Coordinator stale done/release diagnostics were emitted before returning the existing `404 not_found`/`403 forbidden` responses. If the diagnostic cluster-log call unexpectedly raised, the worker could miss the response even though the coordinator had already classified the report.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`

- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

- Unknown completion reports now use the safe coordinator cluster-log wrapper.
- Unknown release reports now use the safe coordinator cluster-log wrapper.
- Done owner-mismatch diagnostics now use the same safe wrapper.
- Existing `404 not_found` and `403 forbidden` response payloads are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-10---coordinator-shutdown-cluster-log-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Coordinator Shutdown Cluster-Log Guardrail

### Root Problem

Coordinator shutdown emitted the `coordinator_stopped` cluster-log diagnostic before stopping the stale-job reaper, closing the HTTP server, stopping mDNS, and saving final in-flight state. If that best-effort diagnostic unexpectedly raised, shutdown teardown could be skipped.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

- Routed the shutdown `coordinator_stopped` diagnostic through the coordinator safe cluster-log wrapper.
- Preserved successful shutdown cluster-log event content.
- Preserved HTTP server close, mDNS stop, stale-reaper join, and final in-flight registry save behavior.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-10---coordinator-claim-handoff-cluster-log-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Coordinator Claim-Handoff Cluster-Log Guardrail

### Root Problem

Successful `/api/claim` handling emitted the `claim_handed` cluster-log diagnostic after the registry claim and persistence attempt, but before returning the claim response to the worker. If that best-effort diagnostic unexpectedly raised, the coordinator could record a claim while the worker never received the job details.

<a id="2026-05-10---coordinator-reclaimed-heartbeat-cluster-log-guardrail"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Coordinator Reclaimed-Heartbeat Cluster-Log Guardrail

### Root Problem

When a worker heartbeat arrived for a job the coordinator had already reclaimed, the coordinator emitted a `heartbeat_reclaimed` cluster-log diagnostic before returning the `reclaimed` heartbeat response. If that diagnostic unexpectedly raised, the worker could miss the abort signal and continue encoding until another failure path intervened.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`

<a id="2026-05-10---coordinator-terminal-done-cluster-log-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Coordinator Terminal Done Cluster-Log Guardrails

### Root Problem

Terminal `/api/done` bookkeeping emitted completed/failed cluster-log diagnostics before saving final in-flight state and before returning the worker `ok` response. If those best-effort diagnostics unexpectedly raised, the coordinator could already have completed the registry entry while skipping final persistence, queue-removal policy, or the worker acknowledgement.

<a id="2026-05-10---worker-crash-recovery-cluster-log-wrapper-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Worker Crash-Recovery Cluster-Log Wrapper Cleanup

### Root Problem

Worker crash recovery already guarded `pending_done_recovered` and `crash_recovered` cluster-log diagnostics with local `try/except/finally` blocks, but those paths used custom warning text instead of the shared worker safe cluster-log wrapper. That left the last worker-side direct cluster-log calls outside the common best-effort diagnostic path.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`

- Routed `pending_done_recovered` through `WorkerDispatcher._safe_log_cluster_event()`.
- Routed `crash_recovered` through the same safe wrapper.
- Preserved accepted-report worker-state cleanup after cluster-log failures.
- Preserved successful crash-recovery event content and coordinator reporting behavior.
